A.3
Construction of the probabilistic quantum comb  \{\mathcal{T}_{I},\mathcal{T}_{O}\}
Figure 9:
The supplementary figure to explain the calculation of  \psi_{\mathrm{out}}  in Eqs. (
181
) – (
185
).
The subscripts on  \mathrm{CG}^{\pm}  are omitted for simplicity.
The operator  \psi_{\mathrm{out}}  is represented by the diagram (a).
By commuting the CG transforms  \mathrm{CG}^{+}  with the inverse of the dual CG transforms  (\mathrm{CG}^{-})^{\dagger} , we obtain (b), where the sequence of the CG transforms corresponds to the quantum Schur transform  \mathrm{Sch}_{n+1,D} , and we can apply Lemma
for the sequence of the inverse of the dual CG transforms.
We show that this construction gives the Choi operator  T_{I}  given in Eq. (
71
).
To this end, it is sufficient to check that
\displaystyle\psi_{\mathrm{out}}\coloneqq J_{\Gamma^{(n+1)}_{I}}\star J_{\Gamma^{(n)}}\star\cdots\star J_{\Gamma^{(1)}}\star\psi_{\mathrm{in}}
(178)
for any pure state  \psi_{\mathrm{in}}=\outerproduct{\psi_\mathrm{in}}{\psi_\mathrm{in}}  for  \ket{\psi_\mathrm{in}}\in\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n} 
satisfies
\displaystyle\psi_{\mathrm{out}}=T_{I}\star\psi_{\mathrm{in}}.
(179)
Suppose  \ket{\psi_\mathrm{in}}  is given in the Schur basis as
\displaystyle\ket{\psi_\mathrm{in}}=\bigoplus_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\sum_{u,i}a_{\mu,u,i}\ket{\mu,u}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mu}^{(D)}}\otimes\ket{\mu,i}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}}.
(180)
The operator  \psi_{\mathrm{out}}  is evaluated as
\displaystyle\psi_{\mathrm{out}}=\Tr_{\mathcal{U}_{n+1}^{(d)}\mathcal{T}_{n+1}\mathcal{U}_{n+1}^{(D)}}[\outerproduct{\psi_\mathrm{out}'}{\psi_\mathrm{out}'}]
(181)
using the vector  \ket{\psi_\mathrm{out}'}\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\otimes\mathcal{U}_{n+1}^{(d)}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{U}_{n+1}^{(D)}  given by (see also Fig.
9
):
\displaystyle\ket{\psi_\mathrm{out}'}
\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{n}[((\mathrm{CG}^{-}_{n,n-i,d})^{\dagger}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{U}_{i+1}^{(D)}})(P_{t_{i+1}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{U}_{i}^{(d)}\mathcal{T}_{i}\mathcal{U}_{i+1}^{(D)}})(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{U}_{i}^{(d)}}\otimes\mathrm{CG}^{+}_{i+1,D})\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{i}\mathcal{O}_{i+2}\cdots\mathcal{O}_{n}}]
\displaystyle\times(\ket{\psi_\mathrm{in}}\otimes\ket{\phi^+_d}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{U}_{1}^{(d)}})
(182)
\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{n}[(\mathrm{CG}^{-}_{n,n-i,d})^{\dagger}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{i-1}\mathcal{T}_{i+2}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{n+1}\mathcal{U}_{n+1}^{(D)}}]
\displaystyle\times[(P_{0})_{\mathcal{T}_{n+1}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{n}\mathcal{U}_{n+1}^{(D)}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{U}_{1}^{(d)}\mathcal{U}_{n+1}^{(D)}}][(\mathrm{Sch}_{n+1,D})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}\to\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{n+1}\mathcal{U}_{n+1}^{(D)}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{U}_{1}^{(d)}}](\ket{\psi_\mathrm{in}}\otimes\ket{\phi^+_d}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{U}_{1}^{(d)}})
(183)
\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{n}[(\mathrm{CG}^{-}_{n,n-i,d})^{\dagger}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{i-1}\mathcal{T}_{i+2}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{n+1}\mathcal{U}_{n+1}^{(D)}}]
\displaystyle\times\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{u,i}a_{\mu,u,i}\ket{u}_{\mathcal{U}_{n+1}^{(D)}}\otimes\ket{\mu_i^{(1)}\cdots\mu_i^{(n+1)}}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{n+1}}\otimes\ket{\phi^+_d}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{U}_{1}^{(d)}}
(184)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{u,i}{a_{\mu,u,i}\over\sqrt{m_{\mu}^{(d)}}}\sum_{u^{\prime}}(\ket{\mu,u'}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mu}^{(d)}}\otimes\ket{\mu,i}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}})_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}\otimes\ket{u'}_{\mathcal{U}_{n+1}^{(d)}}\otimes\ket{\mu}_{\mathcal{T}_{n+1}}\otimes\ket{u}_{\mathcal{U}_{n+1}^{(D)}},
(185)
where  \prod_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}  for operators  X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}  represents  X_{n}\cdots X_{1} , and we recursively use Lemma
.
Therefore, the operator  \psi_{\mathrm{out}}  is evaluated as
\displaystyle\psi_{\mathrm{out}}
\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{u,i,i^{\prime}}a_{\mu,u,i}a_{\mu,u,i^{\prime}}^{*}{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mu}^{(d)}}\over m_{\mu}^{(d)}}\otimes\outerproduct{\mu, i}{\mu,i'}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}}
(186)
\displaystyle=T_{I}\star\psi_{\mathrm{in}},
(187)
where we use Eq. (
74
).
We can also check that the Choi operator  T_{O}\coloneqq J_{\Gamma^{(n+1)}_{O}}\star J_{\Gamma^{(n)}}\star\cdots\star J_{\Gamma^{(1)}}  satisfies the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry:
\displaystyle[T_{O},U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}\otimes U^{\prime\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]=0\quad\forall U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(d),U^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D).
(188)
A.4
Proof of Theorem
4
: Construction of a sequential isometry adjointation protocol
First, we show that the probabilistic quantum comb  \{\mathcal{T}_{I},\mathcal{T}_{O}\}  satisfies
\displaystyle T_{I}\star|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}=\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U|{UV^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{UV^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\otimes|{U}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}+(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V})^{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\Sigma
(189)
for all  V\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D) , where  \differential U  is the Haar measure on  \mathbb{U}(d)  and  \Sigma  is defined in Eq. (
92
).
By definition (
71
) of  T_{I} , it satisfies
\displaystyle[T_{I},\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}\otimes U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]=0\quad\forall U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D).
(190)
Therefore,
\displaystyle T_{I}\star|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
\displaystyle=(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}})(T_{I})\star|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
(191)
\displaystyle=(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{P}})(T_{I}\star|{U^{\prime T}V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U^{\prime T}V}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}})
(192)
Since  U^{\prime T}V=V  holds for  U^{\prime T}=\mathds{1}_{{\mathrm{Im}}V}\oplus U^{\prime\prime}_{({\mathrm{Im}}V)^{\perp}}  using  U^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D-d) , we obtain
\displaystyle[T_{I}\star|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}},\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(\mathds{1}_{{\mathrm{Im}}V}\oplus U^{\prime\prime}_{({\mathrm{Im}}V)^{\perp}})^{T}_{\mathcal{P}}]=0\quad\forall U^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D-d).
(193)
Therefore,  T_{I}\star|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}  satisfies
\displaystyle T_{I}\star|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}=
\displaystyle[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V}^{T})_{\mathcal{P}}](T_{I}\star|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}})[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V}^{T})_{\mathcal{P}}]
\displaystyle+(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V})^{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\Sigma,
(194)
where  \Sigma\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\mathcal{I}^{n})  is given by
\displaystyle\Sigma={1\over D-d}T_{I}\star|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\star(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V})_{\mathcal{P}}.
(195)
Since  \Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V}=VV^{\dagger}  holds, the first term in Eq. (
192
) can be evaluated as
\displaystyle[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V}^{T})_{\mathcal{P}}](T_{I}\star|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}})[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V}^{T})_{\mathcal{P}}]
\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d_{\mu}}{(E^{\mu,d}_{ij})_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}\over{m_{\mu}^{(d)}}}\otimes(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{O}^{n}}\otimes\mathcal{V}^{*}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathcal{P}}\circ\mathcal{V}^{T}_{\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}})(E^{\mu,D}_{ij})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\star|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
(196)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d_{\mu}}{(E^{\mu,d}_{ij})_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}\over{m_{\mu}^{(d)}}}\otimes(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes\mathcal{V}^{*}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathcal{P}})\circ\mathcal{V}^{T\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}\to\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{I}^{n}}(E^{\mu,D}_{ij})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
(197)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d_{\mu}}{(E^{\mu,d}_{ij})_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}\over{m_{\mu}^{(d)}}}\otimes(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes\mathcal{V}^{*}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathcal{P}})(E^{\mu,d}_{ij})_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{I}^{n}}
(198)
\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{V}^{*}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathcal{P}})(|{U}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}})\otimes|{U}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U}|_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}^{\otimes n}
(199)
\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U|{UV^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{UV^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\otimes|{U}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U}|_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}^{\otimes n},
(200)
where  \mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}  is a Hilbert spaces given by  \mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbb{C}^{d} ,  \differential U  is the Haar measure on  \mathbb{U}(d) , and we have utilized the following relations:
\displaystyle V^{\dagger\otimes n+1}E^{\mu,D}_{ij}V^{\otimes n+1}
\displaystyle=\begin{cases}E^{\mu,d}_{ij}&(\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1})\\
0&(\mu\notin\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1})\end{cases},
(201)
\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U|{U}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{I}^{n},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}^{\otimes n+1}
\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d_{\mu}}{(E^{\mu,d}_{ij})_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}\otimes(E^{\mu,d}_{ij})_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{I}^{n}}\over{m_{\mu}^{(d)}}},
(202)
the former of which follows from the decomposition of  V^{\otimes n+1}  shown in Eq. (
59
) and the definition (
60
) of  E^{\mu,d}_{ij} , and the latter of which is shown in Ref.
[
29
]
.
The operator  \Sigma  defined in Eq. (
195
) is shown to be the same as Eq. (
92
) by the following calculation:
\displaystyle\Sigma
\displaystyle={1\over D-d}T_{I}\star|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\star(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V})_{\mathcal{P}}
\displaystyle={1\over D-d}\left[(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathcal{V}^{T\otimes n}_{\mathcal{O}^{n}\to\mathcal{I}^{n}})(\Tr_{\mathcal{P}}T_{I})-\Tr_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}}\circ(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}\otimes\mathcal{V}^{T\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}\to\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{I}^{n}})(T_{I})\right]
(203)
\displaystyle={1\over D-d}\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+_{d}\square}\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\lambda}}\left[{m_{\mu}^{(D)}\over m_{\lambda}^{(D)}}-{m_{\mu}^{(d)}\over m_{\lambda}^{(d)}}\right](E^{\lambda,d}_{ab})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes{(E^{\mu,d}_{a^{\lambda}_{\mu}b^{\lambda}_{\mu}})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\over m_{\mu}^{(d)}},
(204)
where we have utilized Lemma
and Eq. (
201
).
To calculate it further, we employ the dimension formula of the irreducible representation  \mathcal{U}_{\mu}^{(D)}  of  \mathbb{U}(D)  given by Eq. (
57
).
In particular, for  \mu\in\lambda+\square , the ratio of  m_{\mu}^{(d)}  and  m_{\lambda}^{(d)}  is given by
\displaystyle{m_{\mu}^{(d)}\over m_{\lambda}^{(d)}}=(d+j-i){\mathrm{hook}(\lambda)\over\mathrm{hook}(\mu)},
(205)
where  (i,j)  is a coordinate of the box added to obtain  \mu  from  \lambda , i.e.,
\displaystyle{m_{\mu}^{(D)}\over m_{\lambda}^{(D)}}-{m_{\mu}^{(d)}\over m_{\lambda}^{(d)}}
\displaystyle=(D-d){\mathrm{hook}(\lambda)\over\mathrm{hook}(\mu)}
(206)
holds.
Thus, we obtain Eq. (
189
).
We evaluate the Choi operator of  \mathcal{C}_{a}[\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}]  given by
\displaystyle J_{\mathcal{C}_{a}[\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}]}
\displaystyle=C^{\prime}\star T_{a}\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
(207)
to show Theorem
4
. From Eq. (
189
), it is given by
\displaystyle J_{\mathcal{C}_{I}[\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}]}
\displaystyle=C^{\prime}\star\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U|{UV_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{UV_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\otimes|{U}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}+(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})^{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes C^{\prime}\star\Sigma.
(208)
Since  C^{\prime}  is the Choi operator of the covariant unitary inversion protocol, we obtain
[
29
]
\displaystyle C^{\prime}\star|{U}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}={d^{2}F_{\mathrm{UI}}-1\over d^{2}-1}|{U^{-1}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U^{-1}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{F}}+{d^{2}(1-F_{\mathrm{UI}})\over d^{2}-1}{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}.
(209)
Thus, the first term in Eq. (
208
) is given by
\displaystyle C^{\prime}\star\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U|{UV_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{UV_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\otimes|{U}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U|{UV_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{UV_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\star\left[{d^{2}F_{\mathrm{UI}}-1\over d^{2}-1}|{U^{-1}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U^{-1}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{F}}+{d^{2}(1-F_{\mathrm{UI}})\over d^{2}-1}{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\right]
(210)
\displaystyle={d^{2}F_{\mathrm{UI}}-1\over d^{2}-1}|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{F}}+{d^{2}(1-F_{\mathrm{UI}})\over d^{2}-1}{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}\otimes(\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}^{T})_{\mathcal{P}}.
(211)
Since  C^{\prime}  and  \Sigma  satisfy the  \mathbb{U}(d)  symmetry, namely,
\displaystyle[C^{\prime},U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}]
\displaystyle=0,
(212)
\displaystyle[\Sigma,U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}]
\displaystyle=0
(213)
for all  U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(d) ,  C^{\prime}\star\Sigma  satisfies
\displaystyle[C^{\prime}\star\Sigma,U^{\prime}_{\mathcal{F}}]=0
(214)
for all  U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(d) , i.e., it is proportional to the identity operator. Thus, the second term in Eq. (
208
) is given by
\displaystyle(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})^{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes C^{\prime}\star\Sigma
\displaystyle=(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})^{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\Tr[C^{\prime}\star\Sigma]{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}
(215)
\displaystyle=(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})^{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\Tr[\Tr_{\mathcal{F}}(C^{\prime})\Sigma]{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d},
(216)
where we utilize the fact that  \Sigma=\Sigma^{T} . Combining Eqs. (
208
), (
211
), and (
216
), we obtain
\displaystyle J_{\mathcal{C}_{I}[\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}]}
\displaystyle={d^{2}F_{\mathrm{UI}}-1\over d^{2}-1}|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{F}}+{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}\otimes\left[{d^{2}(1-F_{\mathrm{UI}})\over d^{2}-1}(\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}^{T})_{\mathcal{P}}+\alpha_{C^{\prime}}(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})^{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\right],
(217)
where  \alpha_{C^{\prime}}  is defined by
\displaystyle\alpha_{C^{\prime}}\coloneqq\Tr[\Tr_{\mathcal{F}}(C^{\prime})\Sigma].
(218)
Therefore, we obtain
\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{I}[V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}](\rho_{\mathrm{in}})
\displaystyle={d^{2}F_{\mathrm{UI}}-1\over d^{2}-1}\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}(\rho_{\mathrm{in}})+{\mathds{1}_{d}\over d}\Tr\left\{\left[{d^{2}(1-F_{\mathrm{UI}})\over d^{2}-1}\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}+\alpha_{C^{\prime}}(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})\right]\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\right\}.
(219)
Since  \mathcal{C}_{I}[V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}]+\mathcal{C}_{O}[V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}]  is a CPTP map and
\displaystyle\Tr\mathcal{C}_{I}[V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}](\rho_{\mathrm{in}})=\Tr\left\{\left[\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}+\alpha_{C^{\prime}}(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})\right]\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\right\}
(220)
holds,  \Tr\mathcal{C}_{O}[V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}](\rho_{\mathrm{in}})=(1-\alpha_{C^{\prime}})\Tr[(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]  holds. Since  \mathcal{C}_{O}[V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}]  is a CP map, we obtain
\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{O}[V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}](\rho_{\mathrm{in}})=\mathcal{C}_{O}[V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}](\Pi_{({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}})^{\perp}}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\Pi_{({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}})^{\perp}}).
(221)
Due to the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetries of  C^{\prime}  and  T_{O} ,
\displaystyle[C^{\prime}\star T_{O},U_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\otimes n+1}\otimes U_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}^{\prime\prime\otimes n+1}]=0\quad\forall U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(d),U^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D)
(222)
holds. Thus, we obtain
\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{O}[V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}](\rho_{\mathrm{in}})=\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{C}_{O}[(U^{\prime\prime}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime})^{\otimes n}]\circ\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}(\rho_{\mathrm{in}})\quad\forall U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(d),U^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D).
(223)
In particular, for all  U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(d) , we take  U^{\prime\prime}=U^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}\oplus\mathds{1}_{({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}})^{\perp}}  for  U^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(d)  such that  U^{\prime\prime}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime}=V_{\mathrm{in}} . From Eqs. (
221
) and (
223
), we obtain
\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{O}[V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}](\rho_{\mathrm{in}})=\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{C}_{O}[V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}](\rho_{\mathrm{in}})\quad\forall U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(d),
(224)
thus,  \mathcal{C}_{O}[V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}](\rho_{\mathrm{in}})\propto{\mathds{1}_{d}\over d} . From Eq. (
221
), we obtain
\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{O}[V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}](\rho_{\mathrm{in}})={\mathds{1}_{d}\over d}(1-\alpha_{C^{\prime}})\Tr[(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})\rho_{\mathrm{in}}].
(225)
Thus, we obtain
\displaystyle\epsilon=\max\{\alpha_{C^{\prime}},1-F_{\mathrm{UI}}\}.
(226)
A.5
Proof of Theorem
5
: Construction of a parallel isometry adjointation protocol
The parallel protocol shown in Theorem
5
corresponds to the special case of the sequential protocol such that the Choi operator of the original untiary inversion protocol is given by
\displaystyle C^{\prime}=\phi_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{A}}\star\sum_{i}(M_{i}^{T})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\mathcal{A}}\otimes(J_{\mathcal{R}_{i}})_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{F}}.
(227)
Since the Choi operator of a CPTP map  \mathcal{R}_{i}  satisfies  \Tr_{\mathcal{F}}(J_{\mathcal{R}_{i}})_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{F}}=\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}} , we obtain
\displaystyle\Tr_{\mathcal{F}}(C^{\prime})
\displaystyle=\phi_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{A}}\star\sum_{i}(M_{i}^{T})_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\mathcal{A}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}
(228)
\displaystyle=\phi_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{A}}\star\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\mathcal{A}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}
(229)
\displaystyle=\Tr_{\mathcal{A}}(\phi)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}.
(230)
Then,  \alpha_{C^{\prime}}  shown in Eq. (
91
) reduces to  \alpha_{\phi}  given by
\displaystyle\alpha_{\phi}
\displaystyle\coloneqq\Tr[\Tr_{\mathcal{A}}(\phi)\Tr_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}(\Sigma)]
(231)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\Tr[\Tr_{\mathcal{A}}(\phi)\Pi_{\lambda}^{(d)}]\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+_{d}\square}{\mathrm{hook}(\lambda)\over\mathrm{hook}(\mu)}
(232)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\Tr[\Tr_{\mathcal{A}}(\phi)\Pi_{\lambda}^{(d)}]\left[1-\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+\square\setminus\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}{\mathrm{hook}(\lambda)\over\mathrm{hook}(\mu)}\right],
(233)
where we have utilized Lemma
, Eq. (
92
), and the equality
\displaystyle\sum_{\nu\in\lambda+\square}{\mathrm{hook}(\lambda)\over\mathrm{hook}(\nu)}=1,
(234)
shown as follows. From Lemma
,
\displaystyle E_{aa}^{\lambda,D}\otimes\mathds{1}_{D}=\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+_{D}\square}E_{a^{\lambda}_{\mu}a^{\lambda}_{\mu}}^{\mu,D}
(235)
holds for  a\in\{1,\ldots,d_{\lambda}\} . By taking the trace of the both-hand side, we obtain
\displaystyle Dm_{\lambda}^{(D)}=\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+_{D}\square}m_{\mu}^{(D)}.
(236)
By using Eqs. (
57
) and (
236
), we show Eq. (
234
) as
\displaystyle\sum_{\nu\in\lambda+\square}{\mathrm{hook}(\lambda)\over\mathrm{hook}(\nu)}
\displaystyle=\lim_{D\to\infty}{\sum_{\nu\in\lambda+_{D}\square}m_{\nu}^{(D)}\over Dm_{\lambda}^{(D)}}
(237)
\displaystyle=1.
(238)
Thus, the approximation error of isometry adjointation is given by
\displaystyle\epsilon=\max\{\alpha_{\phi},1-F_{\mathrm{est}}\}.
(239)
A.6
Reduction to isometry inversion and universal error detection
A.6.1
Deterministic isometry inversion
By discarding the measurement outcome of the isometry adjointation protocol shown in Eqs. (
219
) and (
224
), we obtain the following protocol:
\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}]=\mathcal{C}_{I}[\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}]+\mathcal{C}_{O}[\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}]
(240)
This protocol satisfies
\displaystyle\mathcal{C}[\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}]\circ\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}(\rho_{\mathrm{in}})={d^{2}F_{\mathrm{UI}}-1\over d^{2}-1}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}+{\mathds{1}_{d}\over d}\left(1-{d^{2}F_{\mathrm{UI}}-1\over d^{2}-1}\right)\Tr(\rho_\mathrm{in}).
(241)
Thus, the worst-case channel fidelity of isometry inversion is given by
\displaystyle F_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathcal{C}[\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}]\circ\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}},\mathds{1}_{d})=F_{\mathrm{UI}}.
(242)
A.6.2
Probabilistic exact isometry inversion
The construction of deterministic exact unitary inversion protocol can be rewritten in terms of the Choi operator as
\displaystyle C^{\prime}\star|{U_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}\approx|{U_{\mathrm{in}}^{-1}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U_{\mathrm{in}}^{-1}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{F}}\quad\forall U_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{U}(d)
\displaystyle\Longrightarrow T\star C^{\prime}\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{P}}
\displaystyle\hskip 24.0pt={d^{2}F_{\mathrm{UI}}-1\over d^{2}-1}|{\mathds{1}_{d}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{\mathds{1}_{d}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{F}}+{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{P}}\over d}\left(1-{d^{2}F_{\mathrm{UI}}-1\over d^{2}-1}\right)\quad\forall V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D),
(243)
where  \mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}  is a Hilbert space given by  \mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}=\mathbb{C}^{d} .
By replacing  C^{\prime}  with the probabilistic exact unitary inversion comb  \{C^{\prime}_{S},C^{\prime}_{F}\}  with the success probability  p_{\mathrm{UI}}  satisfying
\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{S}\star|{U_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}=p_{\mathrm{UI}}|{U_{\mathrm{in}}^{-1}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U_{\mathrm{in}}^{-1}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{F}}\quad\forall U_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{U}(d),
(244)
we obtain the probabilistic exact isometry inversion protocol given by
\displaystyle T\star C^{\prime}_{S}\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{P}}=p_{\mathrm{UI}}|{\mathds{1}_{d}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{\mathds{1}_{d}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{F}}
\displaystyle\forall V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D).
(245)
A.6.3
Universal error detection
By discarding the output state of the isometry adjointation protocol shown in Eqs. (
219
) and (
224
), we obtain the following protocol:
\displaystyle\Tr(\Pi_a \rho_\mathrm{in})=\Tr\circ\mathcal{C}_{a}[\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}](\rho_{\mathrm{in}}),
(246)
This protocol satisfies
\displaystyle\Pi_{I}
\displaystyle=\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}+\alpha_{C^{\prime}}(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}),
(247)
\displaystyle\Pi_{O}
\displaystyle=(1-\alpha_{C^{\prime}})(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}).
(248)
Since  C^{\prime}  corresponds to  C^{\prime}=C^{\prime\prime}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{n}\mathcal{F}}\over d}  using  C^{\prime\prime}  shown in Corollary
7
,  \alpha_{C^{\prime}}  is given by
\displaystyle\alpha_{C}^{\prime}=\Tr(C''\Sigma').
(249)
Appendix B
Analysis on the optimal protocols
B.1
Proof of Theorem
8
:  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry of the tasks
We show Theorem
8
using the idea of a twirling map
[
110
]
similarly to Refs.
[
27
,
29
]
. Assume that the Choi operators (
121
)
achieve the optimal performances in parallel, sequential or general protocols including indefinite causal order. We define the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D) -twirled
Choi operators by
\displaystyle\begin{cases}C^{\prime}\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{U}^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}(C)&(\mathrm{deterministic\;isometry\;inversion})\\
C^{\prime}_{a}\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{U}^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}(C_{a})&(\mathrm{probabilistic\;exact\;isometry\;inversion})\\
C^{\prime}_{a}\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{U}^{\prime\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}(C_{a})&(\mathrm{universal\;error\;detection})\\
C^{\prime}_{a}\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{U}^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}(C_{a})&(\mathrm{isometry\;adjointation})\end{cases},
(250)
where  \differential U^{\prime}  and  \differential U^{\prime\prime}  are the Haar measures of  \mathbb{U}(d)  and  \mathbb{U}(D) , respectively.
Then, these operators satisfy  C^{\prime}_{a}\geq 0  and  C^{\prime}=\sum_{a}C^{\prime}_{a}\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)} , and the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry given by
\displaystyle\begin{cases}[C^{\prime},U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes U^{\prime\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]=0&(\mathrm{deterministic\;isometry\;inversion})\\
[C^{\prime}_{a},U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes U^{\prime\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]=0\quad\forall a\in\{S,F\}&(\mathrm{probabilistic\;exact\;isometry\;inversion})\\
[C^{\prime}_{a},U^{\prime\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes U^{\prime\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]=0\quad\forall a\in\{I,O\}&(\mathrm{universal\;error\;detection})\\
[C^{\prime}_{a},U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes U^{\prime\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]=0\quad\forall a\in\{I,O\}&(\mathrm{isometry\;adjointation})\end{cases}
(251)
for all  U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(d)  and  U^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D) . Then, we can show Theorem
8
by showing that the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D) -twirling does not decrease performances of each task. We show this in the following subsections.
B.1.1
Deterministic isometry inversion
The worst-case fidelity  F_{\mathrm{worst}}  is given using the Choi operator  C  of the quantum superchannel by
\displaystyle F_{\mathrm{worst}}
\displaystyle=\inf_{V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}F_{\mathrm{ch}}[\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n})\circ\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}},\mathds{1}_{d}]
(252)
\displaystyle={1\over d^{2}}\inf_{V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}\Tr[C\star(|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{P}}\otimes|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}})|{\mathds{1}_{d}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{\mathds{1}_{d}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{F}}]
(253)
\displaystyle={1\over d^{2}}\inf_{V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}\Tr[C(|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}|_{\mathcal{F}\mathcal{P}}\otimes|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}})].
(254)
Then, the worst-case channel fidelity of the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D) -twirled Choi operator is given by
\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{\mathrm{worst}}
\displaystyle={1\over d^{2}}\inf_{V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}
\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt\Tr[\mathcal{U}^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}(C)(|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}|_{\mathcal{F}\mathcal{P}}\otimes|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}})]
(255)
\displaystyle\geq{1\over d^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}\inf_{V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}
\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt\Tr[C\mathcal{U}^{\prime\dagger\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\dagger\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}(|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}|_{\mathcal{F}\mathcal{P}}\otimes|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}})]
(256)
\displaystyle={1\over d^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}\inf_{V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}
\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt\Tr[C(|{U^{\prime\prime\dagger}V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}U^{\prime*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U^{\prime\prime\dagger}V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}U^{\prime*}}|_{\mathcal{F}\mathcal{P}}\otimes|{U^{\prime\prime\dagger}V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}U^{\prime*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U^{\prime\prime\dagger}V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}U^{\prime*}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}})]
(257)
\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}F_{\mathrm{worst}}
(258)
\displaystyle=F_{\mathrm{worst}}.
(259)
B.1.2
Probabilistic exact isometry inversion
Suppose a Choi operator  C_{S}  satisfies
\displaystyle C_{S}\star(|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{P}}\otimes|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}})=p|{\mathds{1}_{d}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{\mathds{1}_{d}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{F}}\quad\forall V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D).
(260)
Then, the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D) -twirled Choi operator also satisfies
\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{S}\star(|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{P}}\otimes|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}})
\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{U}^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}(C_{S})\star(|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{P}}\otimes|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}})
(261)
\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}C_{S}\star\mathcal{U}^{\prime T\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime T\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}(|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{P}}\otimes|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}})
(262)
\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}C_{S}\star(|{U^{\prime\prime T}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U^{\prime\prime T}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{P}}\otimes|{U^{\prime\prime T}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U^{\prime\prime T}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}})
(263)
\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}p|{\mathds{1}_{d}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{\mathds{1}_{d}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{F}}
(264)
\displaystyle=p|{\mathds{1}_{d}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{\mathds{1}_{d}}|_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{F}},
(265)
i.e., it implements isometry inversion with success probability  p .
B.1.3
Universal error detection
Suppose a supermap  \mathcal{C}_{I}  satisfies the one-sided error condition given by
\displaystyle\Tr[\mathcal{C}_{I}(\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n})\rho_{\mathrm{in}}]=1\quad\forall\rho\in\mathcal{L}({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}),V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D).
(266)
In terms of the Choi operator, this relation is given by
\displaystyle C_{I}\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\star(\rho_{\mathrm{in}})_{\mathcal{P}}=1\quad\forall\rho\in\mathcal{L}({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}),V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D).
(267)
Then, the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D) -twirlied Choi operator also satisfies the one-sided error condition, i.e.,
\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{I}\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\star(\rho_{\mathrm{in}})_{\mathcal{P}}
\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{U}^{\prime\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}(C_{I})\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\star(\rho_{\mathrm{in}})_{\mathcal{P}}
(268)
\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}C_{I}\star\mathcal{U}^{\prime T\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime T\otimes n}_{\mathcal{O}^{n}}(|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}})\star\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime T}(\rho_{\mathrm{in}})_{\mathcal{P}}
(269)
\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}C_{I}\star(|{U^{\prime\prime T}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U^{\prime\prime T}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}})\star\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime T}(\rho_{\mathrm{in}})_{\mathcal{P}}
(270)
\displaystyle=1
(271)
holds for all  \rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathcal{L}({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}})  and  V_{\mathrm{in}}\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D) .
The worst-case operational distance of the original protocol is given by
\displaystyle\delta_{\mathrm{worst}}=\inf_{V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}D_{\mathrm{op}}(\{(C_{a}\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|)^{T}\}_{a},\{\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}},\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}\}).
(272)
Then, the worst-case operation distance of the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D) -twirlied Choi operator is given by
\displaystyle D^{\prime}_{\mathrm{worst}}
\displaystyle=\inf_{V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}D_{\mathrm{op}}\Bigg(\left\{\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}[C_{a}\star(|{U^{\prime\prime T}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U^{\prime\prime T}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime}}|^{\otimes n}]^{T}\right\}_{a},
\displaystyle\hskip 120.0pt\{\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}},\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}\}\Bigg)
(273)
\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}\inf_{V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}D_{\mathrm{op}}\Big([C_{a}\star(|{U^{\prime\prime T}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U^{\prime\prime T}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime}}|^{\otimes n}]^{T},
\displaystyle\hskip 120.0pt\{\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime*}(\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}),\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime*}(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})\}\Big)
(274)
\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}\inf_{V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}D_{\mathrm{op}}\Big([C_{a}\star(|{U^{\prime\prime T}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U^{\prime\prime T}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime}}|^{\otimes n}]^{T},
\displaystyle\hskip 120.0pt\{\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}U^{\prime\prime T}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime}}),\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}U^{\prime\prime T}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime}}\}\Big)
(275)
\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}\delta_{\mathrm{worst}}
(276)
\displaystyle=\delta_{\mathrm{worst}}
(277)
holds, where we use the convexity (
18
) and the unitary group invariance (
19
) of the operational distance.
B.1.4
Isometry adjointation
We introduce the notation to represent the diamond norm of a quantum channel  \Phi  in terms of its Choi operator  J_{\Phi} , i.e., we define  \mathfrak{D}[J_{\Phi}]  by
\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}[J_{\Phi}]\coloneqq\|\Phi\|_{\diamond}.
(278)
Then,  \mathfrak{D}[J_{\Phi}]  satisfies the following properties:
\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}[(U_{\mathcal{I}}\otimes U^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}})J_{\Phi}(U_{\mathcal{I}}\otimes U^{\prime}_{\mathcal{O}})^{\dagger}]=\mathfrak{D}[J_{\Phi}]\quad
\displaystyle\forall U\in\mathbb{U}(\dim\mathcal{I}),U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(\dim\mathcal{O}),
(279)
\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}[aJ_{\Lambda^{(1)}}+bJ_{\Lambda^{(2)}}]\leq a\mathfrak{D}[J_{\Lambda^{(1)}}]+b\mathfrak{D}[J_{\Lambda^{(2)}}]\quad
\displaystyle\forall a,b\geq 0,
(280)
which corresponds to the following properties of the diamond norm:
\displaystyle\|\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\circ\Phi\circ\mathcal{U}\|_{\diamond}=\|\Phi\|_{\diamond}\quad
\displaystyle\forall U\in\mathbb{U}(\dim\mathcal{I}),U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(\dim\mathcal{O}),
(281)
\displaystyle\|a\Lambda^{(1)}+b\Lambda^{(2)}\|_{\diamond}\leq a\|\Lambda^{(1)}\|_{\diamond}+b\|\Lambda^{(2)}\|_{\diamond}\quad
\displaystyle\forall a,b\geq 0,
(282)
The worst-case diamond-norm error  \epsilon  is given using the Choi operator  C_{I}  by
\displaystyle\epsilon=\inf_{V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}\mathfrak{D}[C\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}^{\otimes n}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}-|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{F}}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}_{\mathcal{A}}-{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}\otimes\outerproduct{1}{1}_{\mathcal{A}}],
(283)
where  C^{\prime}\coloneqq C_{I}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}_{\mathcal{A}}+C_{O}\otimes\outerproduct{1}{1}_{\mathcal{A}}  and  \mathcal{A}=\mathbb{C}^{2} .
Thus, the worst-case diamond-norm error of the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D) -twirled Choi operator is given by
\displaystyle\epsilon^{\prime}
\displaystyle=\inf_{V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}\mathfrak{D}[\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{U}^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(C)\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}^{\otimes n}
\displaystyle\hskip 90.0pt-|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{F}}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}_{\mathcal{A}}-{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}\otimes\outerproduct{1}{1}_{\mathcal{A}}]
(284)
\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}\inf_{V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}\mathfrak{D}[\mathcal{U}^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(C)\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}^{\otimes n}
\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt-|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{F}}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}_{\mathcal{A}}-{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}\otimes\outerproduct{1}{1}_{\mathcal{A}}]
(285)
\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}\inf_{V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}\mathfrak{D}[C\star\mathcal{U}^{\prime T\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime T\otimes n}_{\mathcal{O}^{n}}(|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}^{\otimes n})
\displaystyle\hskip 60.0pt-\mathcal{U}^{\prime\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime\dagger}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}}(|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{F}}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}_{\mathcal{A}}+{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}\otimes\outerproduct{1}{1}_{\mathcal{A}})]
(286)
\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}\inf_{V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}\mathfrak{D}[C\star(|{U^{\prime\prime T}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U^{\prime\prime T}V_{\mathrm{in}}U^{\prime}}|_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}^{\otimes n})
\displaystyle\hskip 90.0pt-|{U^{\prime\dagger}V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}U^{\prime\prime*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U^{\prime\dagger}V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}U^{\prime\prime*}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{F}}\otimes\outerproduct{0}{0}_{\mathcal{A}}-{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}\otimes\outerproduct{1}{1}_{\mathcal{A}}]
(287)
\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{U}(D)}\differential U^{\prime\prime}\epsilon
(288)
\displaystyle=\epsilon,
(289)
i.e., it implements isometry adjointation with approximation error  \epsilon^{\prime}\leq\epsilon .
B.2
Proof of Theorem
12
: Optimal parallel protocol for universal error detection
Due to Theorem
10
, the optimal performance  \alpha  of parallel error detection is given by minimizing  \alpha_{\phi}  shown in Theorem
7
:
\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(\mathrm{PAR})}=\min_{\Tr(\phi)=1,\phi\geq 0}\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\Tr(\phi\Pi_\lambda)\left[1-\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+\square\setminus\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}{\mathrm{hook}(\lambda)\over\mathrm{hook}(\mu)}\right].
(290)
The right-hand side has the minimum value
\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(\mathrm{PAR})}=1-\max_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+\square\setminus\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}{\mathrm{hook}(\lambda)\over\mathrm{hook}(\mu)}
(291)
at  \phi={\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{U}_{\lambda}^{(d)}}\over m_{\lambda}^{(d)}}\otimes\mathrm{arb}_{\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}}  for an arbitrary state  \mathrm{arb}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}) , where  \lambda  is given by
\displaystyle\lambda=\arg\max_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+\square\setminus\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}{\mathrm{hook}(\lambda)\over\mathrm{hook}(\mu)}.
(292)
We define  f(\lambda)  for  \lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}  by
\displaystyle f(\lambda)\coloneqq\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+\square\setminus\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}{\mathrm{hook}(\lambda)\over\mathrm{hook}(\mu)},
(293)
and derive the maximum value of  f(\lambda) . We denote the number of boxes in the  i -th row of  \lambda  and  \mu  by  \lambda_{i}  and  \mu_{i} , respectively. By definition of Young diagrams,  \lambda_{i}  satisfies  \lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\lambda_{d}\geq 0  and  \sum_{i}\lambda_{i}=n . If  \lambda_{d}=0 , any Young diagram in the set  \lambda+\square  has depth smaller than or equal to  d , i.e.,  f(\lambda)=0 . If  \lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\lambda_{d}\geq 1  holds, the set  \lambda+\square\setminus\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}  is a one-point set whose element  \mu  is given by  \mu_{i}=\lambda_{i}  for  i\in\{1,\ldots,d\}  and  \mu_{d+1}=1 .
Therefore,  f(\lambda)  is given by
\displaystyle f(\lambda)={\text{hook}(\lambda)\over\text{hook}(\mu)}=\prod_{i=1}^{d}{\lambda_{i}+d-i\over\lambda_{i}+d+1-i}.
(294)
We derive the maximum value of  f(\lambda)  for  \lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{d}  such that  \lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{d}\geq 1  and  \sum_{i}\lambda_{i}=n . We show that  \lambda  giving the maximum value of  f(\lambda)  should satisfy  |\lambda_{i_{1}}-\lambda_{i_{2}}|\leq 1  for any  1\leq i_{1}
1.
There exists  i^{\prime}_{1}\in\{1,\ldots,d\}  such that  \lambda_{i^{\prime}_{1}}\geq\lambda_{i^{\prime}_{1}+1}+2
2.
There exists  i^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{2}\in\{1,\ldots,d\}  such that  i^{\prime}_{1}+1\leq i^{\prime}_{2}-1 ,  \lambda_{i^{\prime}_{1}}\geq\lambda_{i^{\prime}_{1}+1}+1  and  \lambda_{i^{\prime}_{2}-1}\geq\lambda_{i^{\prime}_{2}}+1
If the first condition holds, we define  i^{\prime}_{2}\coloneqq i^{\prime}_{1}+1 . Then,  \kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{d}  defined by
\displaystyle\kappa_{i}=\begin{cases}\kappa_{i}&(i\neq i^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{2})\\
\kappa_{i^{\prime}_{1}}-1&(i=i^{\prime}_{1})\\
\kappa_{i^{\prime}_{2}}+1&(i=i^{\prime}_{2})\end{cases}
(295)
satisfies  \kappa_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\kappa_{d} ,  \sum_{i}\kappa_{i}=n , and
\displaystyle\frac{f(\kappa)}{f(\lambda)}=\frac{\lambda_{i^{\prime}_{1}}+d-i^{\prime}_{i}-1}{\lambda_{i^{\prime}_{1}}+d-i^{\prime}_{1}+1}\frac{\lambda_{i^{\prime}_{2}}+d-i^{\prime}_{2}+2}{\lambda_{i^{\prime}_{2}}+d-i^{\prime}_{2}}>1,
(296)
i.e.,  \lambda  cannot give the maximum value of  f(\lambda) .
Therefore, the Young diagram  \lambda  giving the maximal value of  f(\lambda)  should satisfy  |\lambda_{i_{1}}-\lambda_{i_{2}}|\leq 1  for any  1\leq i_{1}
\displaystyle\lambda_{i}=\begin{cases}k+1&(i\in\{1,\ldots,l\})\\
k&(i\in\{l+1,\ldots,d\})\end{cases},
(297)
where  k\in\mathbb{Z}  and  l\in\{0,\ldots,d-1\}  are defined by  n=kd+l . Then, we obtain
\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(\mathrm{PAR})}=1-\max_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}f(\lambda)={1\over d+k+1}\left(d+{d-l\over d+k-l}\right).
(298)
B.3
Proof of Theorem
14
: Asymptotically optimal parallel protocol for isometry adjointation
For  d=2 , Refs.
[
69
,
65
]
present the maximum-likelihood qubit-unitary estimation protocol achieving the entanglement fidelity  F_{\mathrm{est}}=1-O(n^{-2})  using  n  calls of input unitary operation. Although Refs.
[
69
,
65
]
do not present the explicit form of the probe state, one can utilize the resource state for entanglement-assisted alignment of the reference frames presented in Ref.
[
68
]
to achieve the same asymptotic scaling of the entanglement fidelity  F_{\mathrm{est}}=1-O(n^{-2}) . The resource state presented in Ref.
[
68
]
is given by
\displaystyle\ket{\phi}={2\over\sqrt{n+3}}\sum_{j=0(1/2)}^{n/2}{1\over\sqrt{2j+1}}\sin{(2j+1)\pi\over n+3}\sum_{m=-j}^{j}\ket{jm}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\ket{jm}_{\mathcal{A}},
(299)
where  j  and  m  represents total angular momentum and  z -component of total angular momentum of a  n -qubit system. The summation of  j  starts from  0  if  n  is even and  1/2  if  n  is odd. These values correspond to the Schur basis, where  j  corresponds to the Young diagram  \lambda  whose number of boxes in  i -th row, denoted by  \lambda_{i}  ( i=1,2 ), is determined by  j=(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})/2 , and  m  corresponds to an element in  \mathcal{U}_{\lambda}^{(2)} .
Then, the value  \alpha_{\phi}  shown in Theorem
5
is calculated as
\displaystyle\alpha_{\phi}
\displaystyle={4\over n+3}\sum_{j=0(1/2)}^{n/2}\sin^{2}{(2j+1)\pi\over n+3}\left[1-{({n\over 2}+j+1)({n\over 2}-j)\over({n\over 2}+j+2)({n\over 2}-j+1)}\right].
(300)
To evaluate  \alpha_{\phi}  in the asymptotic limit  n\to\infty , we introduce the variable  x={2j\over n}  and approximate the sum in Eq. (
300
) by the integral over  x  as
\displaystyle\alpha_{\phi}
\displaystyle={4\over n+3}\int_{0}^{1}\differential x{n\over 2}\sin^{2}{(x+{1\over n})\pi\over 1+{3\over n}}\left[1-{(1+x+{2\over n})(1-x)\over(1+x+{4\over n})(1-x+{2\over n})}\right]+O(n^{-2})
(301)
\displaystyle={8\over n}\int_{0}^{1}\differential x{\sin^{2}(x\pi)\over 1-x^{2}}+O(n^{-2})
(302)
\displaystyle\approx\frac{6.2287}{n}+O(n^{-2}),
(303)
where the integral is evaluated by
Mathematica
[
111
]
. Thus, utilizing this estimation method to construct the parallel isometry adjointation protocol, one can achieve
\displaystyle\epsilon=\max\{\alpha_{\phi},1-F_{\mathrm{est}}\}={6.2287\over n}+O(n^{-2}).
(304)
For a higher-dimension  d>2 , Ref.
[
14
]
presents an asymptotically optimal unitary estimation protocol achieving the entanglement fidelity  F_{\mathrm{est}}=1-O(d^{4}n^{-2}) . This protocol utilizes the probe state given by
\displaystyle\ket{\phi}=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathrm{S}_{\mathrm{young}}}\sqrt{{q_{\lambda}\over d_{\lambda}m_{\lambda}^{(d)}}}\sum_{i=1}^{d_{\lambda}}\sum_{u=1}^{m_{\lambda}^{(d)}}(\ket{\lambda,i}_{\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}}\otimes\ket{\lambda,u}_{\mathcal{U}_{\lambda}^{(d)}})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(\ket{\lambda,i}_{\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}}\otimes\ket{\lambda,u}_{\mathcal{U}_{\lambda}^{(d)}})_{\mathcal{A}},
(305)
where  \{q_{\lambda}\}  is a probability distribution over the set  \mathrm{S}_{\mathrm{young}}\subset\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}  defined by
\mathrm{S}_{\mathrm{young}}\coloneqq\{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}|\lambda_{i}=\mu_{0,i}+N(2d-3)+1-(N+1)(i-1)+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\\
\forall i\leq d-1,\exists\tilde{\lambda}\in\{0,\cdots,N-1\}^{d-1}\}.
(308)
Here,  \lambda_{i}  represents the number of boxes in the  i -th row of  \lambda ,  N  is defined by  N\coloneqq\lfloor{1\over 3d-2}({2n\over d-1}+d-2)\rfloor ,  \mu_{0,i}  is defined by  \mu_{0,i}\coloneqq\lfloor{n_{0}\over d}\rfloor+1  for  i\in\{1,\ldots,n_{0}-\lfloor{n_{0}\over d}\rfloor d\}  and  \mu_{0,i}\coloneqq\lfloor{n_{0}\over d}\rfloor  for  i\in\{n_{0}-\lfloor{n_{0}\over d}\rfloor d+1,\ldots,d\} , and  n_{0}  is defined by  n_{0}\coloneqq n-{((3d-2)N-d+2)(d-1)\over 2} . Then, the value  \alpha_{\phi}  shown in Theorem
5
is evaluated as
\displaystyle\alpha_{\phi}
\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathrm{S}_{\mathrm{young}}}q_{\lambda}\left[1-\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+\square\setminus\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}{\mathrm{hook}(\lambda)\over\mathrm{hook}(\mu)}\right]
(309)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathrm{S}_{\mathrm{young}}}q_{\lambda}\left[1-\prod_{i=1}^{d}{\lambda_{i}+d-i\over\lambda_{i}+d-i+1}\right].
(310)
We evaluate  \alpha_{\phi}  in the asymptotic limit  n\to\infty . In this region,  \lambda_{i}  is given by
\displaystyle\lambda_{i}={4n\over 3d}-{2n\over 3d^{2}}(i-1)+O(d,d^{-2}n).
(311)
Thus,  \alpha_{\phi}  is evaluated as follows.
\displaystyle\alpha_{\phi}
\displaystyle=1-\prod_{i=1}^{d}\left[1-{3d\over 2n}{1+O(d^{2}n^{-1},d^{-1})\over 2-{i-1\over d}}\right]
(312)
\displaystyle={3d\over 2n}\sum_{i=1}^{d}{1\over 2-{i-1\over d}}+O(d^{4}n^{-2},dn^{-1}).
(313)
\displaystyle={3d^{2}\over 2n}\int_{0}^{1}{\differential x\over 2-x}+O(d^{4}n^{-2},dn^{-1})
(314)
\displaystyle={3\ln 2\over 2}{d^{2}\over n}+O(d^{4}n^{-2},dn^{-1}).
(315)
Thus, utilizing this estimation method to construct the parallel isometry adjointation protocol, one can achieve
\displaystyle\epsilon=\max\{\alpha_{\phi},1-F_{\mathrm{est}}\}={3\ln 2\over 2}{d^{2}\over n}+O(d^{4}n^{-2},dn^{-1}).
(316)
Appendix C
Proof of Theorem
10
: Optimal construction of isometry inversion, universal error detection, and isometry adjointation protocols
We show Theorem
10
by constructing the parallel or sequential protocol for isometry inversion, universal error detection, and isometry adjointation in the forms shown in Theorems
4
and
5
and Corollaries
6
and
7
achieving the optimal performances among parallel or sequential protocols. To this end, we utilize Theorem
8
to write down the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetric Choi operators of parallel or sequential protocols achieving the optimal performances. Then we construct the protocols in the forms shown in Theorems
4
and
5
and Corollaries
6
and
7
by constructing the corresponding Choi operators.
Suppose  \mathcal{I}_{1}=\cdots=\mathcal{I}_{n}=\mathcal{F}=\mathbb{C}^{d} ,  \mathcal{O}_{1}=\cdots=\mathcal{O}_{n}=\mathcal{P}=\mathbb{C}^{D} ,  \mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{1}=\cdots=\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{n}=\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}^{d} , and we define the joint Hilbert spaces  \mathcal{I}^{n}\coloneqq\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{I}_{i} ,  \mathcal{O}^{n}\coloneqq\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{O}_{i} , and  \mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\coloneqq\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{i} . We assume that
\displaystyle C\in\begin{cases}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F})&(\mathrm{isometry\;inversion},\mathrm{isometry\;adjointation})\\
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P})&(\mathrm{universal\;error\;detection})\end{cases}
(317)
is the Choi operator satisfying the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry (
401
), which can be written as Eqs. (
402
) or (
403
) using  \{C_{\mu\nu}\}  or  \{C_{\lambda\nu}\} . If  C  is the Choi operator of parallel or sequential protocol, i.e.,  C\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)}  for  x\in\{\mathrm{PAR},\mathrm{SEQ}\} , the Choi operator
\displaystyle C^{\prime}\in\begin{cases}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{F})&(\mathrm{isometry\;inversion},\mathrm{isometry\;adjointation})\\
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\prime})&(\mathrm{universal\;error\;detection})\end{cases}
(318)
defined by
\displaystyle C^{\prime}\coloneqq\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d_{\mu}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}\frac{[C_{\mu\nu}]_{ik,jl}}{m_{\mu}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}(E_{ij}^{\mu,d})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes(E_{kl}^{\nu,d})_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}
(319)
for isometry inversion or isometry adjointation, and
\displaystyle C^{\prime}\coloneqq\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\lambda}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}\frac{[C_{\lambda\nu}]_{ak,bl}}{m_{\lambda}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}(E_{ab}^{\lambda,d})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(E_{kl}^{\nu,d})_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}
(320)
for universal error detection, satisfies  C^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)}  [see Eqs. (
411
) and (
412
)]. We utilize this fact to show the construction of the protocols in the forms shown in Theorems
4
and
5
and Corollaries
6
and
7
.
C.1
Probabilistic exact isometry inversion
Assume that  \{C_{S},C_{F}\}\subset\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F})  is the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetric Choi operator of parallel ( x=\mathrm{PAR} ) or sequential ( x=\mathrm{SEQ} ) protocol achieving the optimal success probability  p_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n)  of isometry inversion, which can be written as Eqs. (
484
) and (
485
). Defining  \{C^{\prime}_{S},C^{\prime}_{F}\}\subset\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{F})  by
\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{S}
\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d_{\mu}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}\frac{[S_{\mu\nu}]_{ik,jl}}{m_{\mu}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}(E_{ij}^{\mu,d})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes(E_{kl}^{\nu,d})_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}},
(321)
\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{F}
\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d_{\mu}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}\frac{[F_{\mu\nu}]_{ik,jl}}{m_{\mu}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}(E_{ij}^{\mu,d})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes(E_{kl}^{\nu,d})_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}},
(322)
C^{\prime}_{S},C^{\prime}_{F}\geq 0  and  C^{\prime}\coloneqq C^{\prime}_{S}+C^{\prime}_{F}\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)}  holds. Thus, the corresponding supermap  \{\mathcal{C}_{S},\mathcal{C}_{F}\}  can be implemented in a parallel ( x=\mathrm{PAR} ) or sequential ( x=\mathrm{SEQ} ) protocol. In particular for the case  x=\mathrm{PAR} , since its Choi operator satisfies the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(d)  symmetry, it can be implemented using a delayed input-state protocol
[
28
,
27
]
. Since  \{C_{S},C_{F}\}  achieves the optimal success probability  p_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n) , it satisfies [see Eq. (
483
)]
\displaystyle\Tr(C_S \Omega)
\displaystyle=p_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n),
(323)
\displaystyle\Tr(C_S \Omega)
\displaystyle=\Tr[C_{S}(\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})].
(324)
Defining  \Omega^{\prime}  and  \Xi^{\prime}  by replacing  \mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}  and  D  in Eqs. (
475
) and (
477
) with  \mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}  and  d , respectively,  \{C^{\prime}_{S},C^{\prime}_{F}\}  satisfies
\displaystyle\Tr(C'_S \Omega')
\displaystyle=p_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n),
(325)
\displaystyle\Tr(C'_S \Omega')
\displaystyle=\Tr[C^{\prime}_{S}(\Xi^{\prime}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})],
(326)
which implies  \{C^{\prime}_{S},C^{\prime}_{F}\}  implements probabilistic exact  d -dimensional unitary inversion with success probability  p_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n) . Therefore, we can construct a parallel or sequential protocol achieving the optimal success probability  p_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n)  of isometry inversion using the construction shown in Theorem
6
.
C.2
Deterministic isometry inversion
Assume that  C\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F})  is the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetric Choi operator of parallel ( x=\mathrm{PAR} ) or sequential ( x=\mathrm{SEQ} ) protocol achieving the optimal worst-case channel fidelity  F_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n) , which can be written as Eq. (
402
). Defining  C^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{F})  by Eq. (
319
),  C^{\prime}\geq 0  and  C^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)}  holds. Thus, the corresponding supermap  \mathcal{C}  can be implemented in a parallel or sequential protocol. In particular for the case  x=\mathrm{PAR} , since its Choi operator satisfies the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(d)  symmetry, it can be implemented using an estimation-based protocol
[
29
]
. Since  C  achieves the optimal worst-case channel fidelity  F_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n) , it satisfies [see Eq. (
491
)]
\displaystyle\Tr(C \Omega)
\displaystyle=F_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n).
(327)
Defining  \Omega^{\prime}  by replacing  \mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}  and  D  in Eq. (
475
) with  \mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}  and  d , respectively,  C^{\prime}  satisfies
\displaystyle\Tr(C' \Omega')
\displaystyle=F_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n),
(328)
which implies  C^{\prime}  implements deterministic  d -dimensional unitary inversion with worst-case channel fidelity  p_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n) . Therefore, we can construct a parallel or sequential protocol achieving the optimal worst-case channel fidelity  F_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n)  of isometry inversion using the construction shown in Theorem
6
.
C.3
Universal error detection
Assume that  \{C_{I},C_{O}\}\subset\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P})  is the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetric Choi operator of a parallel ( x=\mathrm{PAR} ) or sequential ( x=\mathrm{SEQ} ) protocol achieving the optimal error  \alpha_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n) , which can be written as Eqs. (
494
) and (
495
). Defining  C^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\prime})  by Eq. (
320
) for  C_{\lambda\nu}\coloneqq I_{\lambda\nu}+O_{\lambda\nu}  for  \lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}  and  \nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1} ,  C^{\prime\prime}\geq 0  and  C^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)}  hold. Characterization of  \mathcal{W}^{(x)}  shown in Eqs. (
399
) and (
400
) for the case  \mathcal{F}=\mathbb{C}  (no global future) and the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(d)  symmetry of  C^{\prime\prime}  implies that  C^{\prime\prime}  can be written as
\displaystyle C^{\prime\prime}=\begin{cases}\phi_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}}&(x=\mathrm{PAR}),\\
C^{\prime}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{n}}&(x=\mathrm{SEQ}),\end{cases}
(329)
where  \phi\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n})  is a quantum state and  C^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\prime n-1}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\prime})  is the Choi operator of a  (n-1) -slot sequential protocol. Since  \{C_{I},C_{O}\}  achieves the optimal error  \alpha_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n) , it satisfies [see Eq.(
493
)]
\displaystyle\Tr(C_I\Sigma)
\displaystyle=\alpha_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n),
(330)
\displaystyle\Tr(C_I\Xi)
\displaystyle=1.
(331)
Since Eq. (
331
) corresponds to the condition
\displaystyle\Tr\;\mathcal{C}_{I}(\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n})(\rho_{\mathrm{in}})=1\quad\forall\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathcal{L}({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}),
(332)
it can be replaced with the equivalent condition
\displaystyle\Tr\;\mathcal{C}_{O}(\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n})(\rho_{\mathrm{in}})=0\quad\forall\rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathcal{L}({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}),
(333)
which can be represented as
\displaystyle\Tr(C_O \Xi)=0.
(334)
Therefore, we obtain [see Eq. (
477
)]
\displaystyle 0
\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\Tr(O_{\lambda\nu} \Xi_{\lambda\nu})
(335)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\lambda\in\nu-\square}\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\lambda}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}{m_{\nu}^{(d)}\over dm_{\nu}^{(D)}m_{\lambda}^{(d)}}[\pi_{\nu}]_{a_{\nu}^{\lambda}k}^{*}[\pi_{\nu}]_{lb_{\nu}^{\lambda}}[O_{\lambda\nu}]_{ba,lk},
(336)
which leads to
\displaystyle\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\lambda}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}[\pi_{\nu}]_{a_{\nu}^{\lambda}k}^{*}[\pi_{\nu}]_{lb_{\nu}^{\lambda}}[O_{\lambda\nu}]_{ba,lk}
\displaystyle=0\quad\forall\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1},\lambda\in\nu-\square.
(337)
Thus, we obtain [see Eq. (
482
)]
\displaystyle\Tr(O_{\lambda\nu} \Sigma_{\lambda\nu})=0\quad\forall\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1},\lambda\in\nu-\square.
(338)
Since Eq. (
482
) reduces to  \Sigma  defined in Eq. (
92
) by replacing  \mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}  and  D  with  \mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}  and  d ,
\displaystyle\Tr(C''\Sigma)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\lambda\in\nu-\square}\Tr(C_{\lambda\nu}\Sigma_{\lambda\nu})
(339)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\lambda\in\nu-\square}\Tr(I_{\lambda\nu}\Sigma_{\lambda\nu})
(340)
\displaystyle\leq\Tr(C_I \Sigma)
(341)
\displaystyle=\alpha_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}
(342)
holds.
Substituting Eq. (
329
) for  x=\mathrm{PAR},\mathrm{SEQ}  to Eq. (
342
), we obtain
\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(\mathrm{PAR})}
\displaystyle\geq\Tr(\phi\Tr_{\mcP'\mcO'^n}(\Sigma))
(343)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\lambda+_{d}\square}\Tr(\phi\Pi_\lambda^{(d)}){\mathrm{hook}(\lambda)\over\mathrm{hook}(\nu)}
(344)
\displaystyle=\alpha_{\phi},
(345)
\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(\mathrm{SEQ})}
\displaystyle\geq\Tr(C' \Tr_{\mcO'_n}(\Sigma))
(346)
\displaystyle=\Tr(C'\Sigma')
(347)
\displaystyle=\alpha_{C^{\prime}},
(348)
where  \alpha_{\phi}  and  \alpha_{C^{\prime}}  are defined in Theorem
7
, respectively.
Thus, the parallel and sequential protocols constructed in Theorem
7
achieves the optimal error  \alpha_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n) .
C.4
Isometry adjointation
Assume that  \{C_{I},C_{O}\}\subset\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F})  is the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetric Choi operator of parallel ( x=\mathrm{PAR} ) or sequential ( x=\mathrm{SEQ} ) protocol achieving the optimal worst-case diamond-norm error  \epsilon_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n)  of isometry adjointation, which can be written as Eqs. (
500
) and (
501
). Defining  C^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{F})  by Eq. (
319
) for  C_{\mu\nu}\coloneqq I_{\mu\nu}+O_{\mu\nu}  for  \mu,\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1} ,  C^{\prime}\geq 0  and  C^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)}  hold. Thus, the corresponding supermap  \mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}  can be implemented by a parallel ( x=\mathrm{PAR} ) or sequential ( x=\mathrm{SEQ} ) protocol. In particular, for the case  x=\mathrm{PAR} , since its Choi operator satisfies the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(d)  symmetry, it can be implemented using a covariant-estimation-based protocol as shown in Fig.
5
(a-1)
[
14
,
29
]
. Since  \{C_{I},C_{O}\}  achieves the optimal worst-case diamond-norm error  \epsilon_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n)  of isometry adjointation, it satisfies [see Eq. (
499
)]
\displaystyle 1-\Tr(C_I\Omega)
\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n),
(349)
\displaystyle\Tr[C_{I}(\Sigma\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]
\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n),
(350)
\displaystyle\Tr[C_{I}(\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]
\displaystyle=1.
(351)
Similarly to Sections
C.2
and
C.3
, defining  \Omega^{\prime}  by replacing  \mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}  and  D  in Eq. (
475
) with  \mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mathcal{O}^{\prime n}  and  d , we obtain
\displaystyle 1-\Tr(C'\Omega')
\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n),
(352)
\displaystyle\Tr[C^{\prime}(\Sigma\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]
\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(x)}(d,D,n).
(353)
For the case  x=\mathrm{PAR} ,  C^{\prime}  can be implemented by a covariant unitary-estimation protocol achieving the average fidelity  F_{\mathrm{est}}=\Tr(C'\Omega')
[
14
,
29
]
.  \Tr[C^{\prime}(\Sigma\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]  can be evaluated by the probe state  \phi\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{A})  of the unitary estimation protocol as
\displaystyle\Tr[C^{\prime}(\Sigma\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]
\displaystyle=\Tr[\Tr_{\mathcal{F}}(C)\Sigma]
(354)
\displaystyle=\Tr(\Tr_\mcA(\phi) \Tr_{\mcP'\mcO'^n}(\Sigma))
(355)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\Tr[\Tr_{\mathcal{A}}(\phi)\Pi_{\lambda}^{(d)}]\left[1-\sum_{\nu\in\lambda+\square\setminus\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}{\mathrm{hook}(\lambda)\over\mathrm{hook}(\nu)}\right]
(356)
\displaystyle=\alpha_{\phi},
(357)
where  \alpha_{\phi}  is defined in Theorem
5
.
Thus, the parallel protocol constructed in Theorem
5
achieves the optimal worst-case diamond-norm error  \epsilon_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(\mathrm{PAR})}(d,D,n) .
For the case  x=\mathrm{SEQ} ,  \Tr(C'\Omega')  represents the worst-case channel fidelity of  d -dimensional unitary inversion, and  \Tr[C^{\prime}(\Sigma\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]  can be evaluated as [see Eq. (
400
)]
\displaystyle\Tr[C^{\prime}(\Sigma\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]
\displaystyle=\Tr[\Tr_{\mathcal{F}}(C^{\prime})\Sigma]
(358)
\displaystyle=\alpha_{C^{\prime}},
(359)
where  \alpha_{C^{\prime}}  is defined in Theorem
4
. Thus, the sequential protocol constructed in Theorem
4
achieves the optimal worst-case diamond-norm error  \epsilon_{\mathrm{opt}}^{(\mathrm{SEQ})}(d,D,n) .
