Appendix E
Semidefinite programming to obtain the optimal transformations of isometry operations with the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry
As shown in Theorem
8
, the optimal protocols for isometry inversion, universal error detection and isometry adjointation can be searched within the Choi operators having the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry. To utilize this symmetry for a numerical search of the optimal protocols, we derive the characterization of the quantum superchannels and the conditions for isometry inversion, universal error detection, and isometry adjointation under the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry. Then, we derive the SDPs giving the optimal performances of these tasks, which are shown below.
E.1
Choi representation of general superchannels and  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry
As shown in Section
4.1
, the Choi operator  C  of a quantum superchannel  \mathcal{C}:\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}[\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}_{i})\to\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{O}_{i})]\to[\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{P})\to\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{F})]  implemented by parallel ( x=\mathrm{PAR} ) and sequential ( x=\mathrm{SEQ} ) protocols, and general superchannels ( x=\mathrm{GEN} ) can be represented by a Choi operator  C\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F})  satisfying
\displaystyle C
\displaystyle\geq 0,
(397)
\displaystyle C
\displaystyle\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)}.
(398)
The set  \mathcal{W}^{(x)}  for  x\in\{\mathrm{PAR},\mathrm{SEQ}\}  are given by
[
50
,
27
]
\displaystyle C\in\mathcal{W}^{(\mathrm{PAR})}
\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\begin{cases}\Tr_{\mathcal{F}}C=\Tr_{\mathcal{O}^{n}}C\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{O}^{n}}/\dim\mathcal{O}^{n}\\
\Tr_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}C=\dim\mathcal{O}^{n}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}}\end{cases},
(399)
\displaystyle C\in\mathcal{W}^{(\mathrm{SEQ})}
\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\Tr_{\mathcal{I}_{i}}C^{(i)}=C^{(i-1)}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{i-1}}\quad\forall i\in\{1,\ldots,n+1\},
(400)
where  \mathcal{O}_{0}  and  \mathcal{I}_{n+1}  are defined by  \mathcal{O}_{0}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}  and  \mathcal{I}_{n+1}\coloneqq\mathcal{F} , and  C^{(i)}  for  i\in\{0,\ldots,n+1\}  are defined by  C^{(n+1)}\coloneqq C ,  C^{(i-1)}\coloneqq\Tr_{\mathcal{O}_{i-1}\mathcal{I}_{i}}C^{(i)}/\dim\mathcal{O}_{i-1}  and  C^{(0)}\coloneqq 1 . The characterization of the set  \mathcal{W}^{(\mathrm{GEN})}  is shown in Ref.
[
112
]
.
We consider the case  \mathcal{I}_{1}=\cdots=\mathcal{I}_{n}=\mathcal{F}=\mathbb{C}^{d},\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{O}_{1}=\cdots=\mathcal{O}_{n}=\mathbb{C}^{D}  (isometry inversion, isometry adjointation) and  \mathcal{I}_{1}=\cdots=\mathcal{I}_{n}=\mathbb{C}^{d},\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{O}_{1}=\cdots=\mathcal{O}_{n}=\mathbb{C}^{D},\mathcal{F}=\mathbb{C}  (universal error detection), and characterize the Choi operator  C\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F})  under the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry given by
\displaystyle\begin{cases}[C,U^{\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]=0&(\mathrm{isometry\;inversion},\mathrm{isometry\;adjointation})\\
[C,U^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]=0&(\mathrm{universal\;error\;detection})\end{cases}
(401)
for all  U\in\mathbb{U}(d)  and  U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D)  (see Theorem
8
). Due to this symmetry, the Choi operator  C  can be written using the operator  E^{\mu,d}_{ij}  introduced in Eq. (
60
) as
\displaystyle C=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d_{\mu}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}\frac{[C_{\mu\nu}]_{ik,jl}}{m_{\mu}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}(E_{ij}^{\mu,d})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes(E_{kl}^{\nu,D})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
(402)
for isometry inversion or isometry adjointation, and
\displaystyle C=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\lambda}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}\frac{[C_{\lambda\nu}]_{ak,bl}}{m_{\lambda}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}(E_{ab}^{\lambda,d})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(E_{kl}^{\nu,D})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
(403)
for universal error detection using a  d_{\mu}d_{\nu}  ( d_{\lambda}d_{\nu} )-dimensional square matrix  C_{\mu\nu}  ( C_{\lambda\nu} ), where  ik  ( ak ) and  jl  ( bl ) are the indices for row and column numbers, respectively. The characterization of quantum superchannels is rewritten in terms of  C_{\mu\nu}  or  C_{\lambda\nu}  as follows. By definition of  E^{\mu,d}_{ij}  in Eq. (
60
), the positivity of  C  is written as
\displaystyle\begin{cases}C_{\mu\nu}\geq 0\quad\forall\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}&(\mathrm{isometry\;inversion},\mathrm{isometry\;adjointation})\\
C_{\lambda\nu}\geq 0\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}&(\mathrm{universal\;error\;detection})\end{cases}.
(404)
Using Lemma
, the condition  C\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)}  for  x\in\{\mathrm{PAR},\mathrm{SEQ}\}  is given by
\displaystyle C\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)}\Longleftrightarrow\begin{cases}\{C_{\mu\nu}\}\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(x)}&(\mathrm{isometry\;inversion},\mathrm{isometry\;adjointation})\\
\{C_{\lambda\nu}\}\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(x)}&(\mathrm{universal\;error\;detection})\end{cases},
(405)
where  \mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(x)}  is given by
\displaystyle\{C_{\mu\nu}\}\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(\mathrm{PAR})}\Longleftrightarrow
\displaystyle\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+_{d}\square}(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}}){C_{\mu\nu}\over m_{\nu}^{(D)}}(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})^{\dagger}=D_{\lambda}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}}\over D^{n+1}}\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}\\
&\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\Tr(C_{\mu\nu})=D^{n+1}\end{aligned}\end{cases},
(406)
\displaystyle\{C_{\mu\nu}\}\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(\mathrm{SEQ})}\Longleftrightarrow
\displaystyle\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &\begin{aligned} \sum_{\lambda\in\gamma+_{d}\square}(X^{\gamma}_{\lambda}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\kappa}}){C_{\lambda\kappa}^{(i)}\over m_{\kappa}^{(D)}}(X^{\gamma}_{\lambda}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\kappa}})^{\dagger}=\sum_{\delta\in\kappa-\square}(\mathds{1}_{d_{\gamma}}\otimes X^{\delta}_{\kappa})^{\dagger}{C_{\gamma\delta}^{(i-1)}\over m_{\delta}^{(D)}}(\mathds{1}_{d_{\gamma}}\otimes X^{\delta}_{\kappa})\\
\forall i\in\{1,\cdots,n+1\},\gamma\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{i-1},\kappa\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{i}\end{aligned}\\
&C_{\emptyset\emptyset}^{(0)}=1\end{aligned}\end{cases}
(407)
for isometry inversion and isometry adjointation, where  D_{\lambda}  for  \lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}  are defined by
\displaystyle D_{\lambda}\coloneqq\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+_{d}\square}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\Tr_{\nu}[(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})C_{\mu\nu}(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})^{\dagger}]
(408)
C^{(i)}_{\lambda\kappa}  for  \lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{i},\kappa\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{i}  are defined by
\displaystyle C^{(i)}_{\lambda\kappa}\coloneqq\begin{cases}C_{\lambda\kappa}&(i=n+1)\\
{1\over D}\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+_{d}\square,\nu\in\kappa+_{D}\square}(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})C_{\mu\nu}^{(i+1)}(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})^{\dagger}&(0\leq i\leq n)\end{cases},
(409)
X^{\gamma}_{\lambda}  for  \lambda\in\gamma+\square,\gamma\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{i-1}  are  d_{\gamma}\times d_{\lambda}  matrices defined by
\displaystyle[X^{\gamma}_{\lambda}]_{c,a}\coloneqq\delta_{c^{\gamma}_{\lambda},a},
(410)
c^{\gamma}_{\lambda}  is the index of the standard tableau  s^{\lambda}_{c^{\gamma}_{\lambda}}  obtained by adding a box  i  to the standard tableau  s^{\gamma}_{c} , and  \emptyset  represents the Young tableau with zero boxes.
The set  \mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(x)}  is given by
\displaystyle\{C_{\lambda\nu}\}\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(\mathrm{PAR})}\Longleftrightarrow
\displaystyle\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &{C_{\lambda\nu}\over m_{\nu}^{(D)}}=D_{\lambda}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}}\over D^{n+1}}\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}\\
&\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\Tr(C_{\lambda\nu})=D^{n}\end{aligned}\end{cases},
(411)
\displaystyle\{C_{\lambda\nu}\}\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(\mathrm{SEQ})}\Longleftrightarrow
\displaystyle\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &{C_{\lambda\nu}\over m_{\nu}^{(D)}}=\sum_{\kappa\in\nu-\square}(\mathds{1}_{d_{\lambda}}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})^{\dagger}{C_{\lambda\kappa}^{(n)}\over m_{\kappa}^{(D)}}(\mathds{1}_{d_{\lambda}}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}\\
&\begin{aligned} \sum_{\lambda\in\gamma+_{d}\square}(X^{\gamma}_{\lambda}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\kappa}}){C_{\lambda\kappa}^{(i)}\over m_{\kappa}^{(D)}}(X^{\gamma}_{\lambda}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\kappa}})^{\dagger}=\sum_{\delta\in\kappa-\square}(\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}}\otimes X^{\delta}_{\kappa})^{\dagger}{C_{\gamma\delta}^{(i-1)}\over m_{\delta}^{(D)}}(\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}}\otimes X^{\delta}_{\kappa})\\
\forall i\in\{1,\cdots,n\},\gamma\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{i-1},\kappa\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{i}\end{aligned}\\
&C_{\emptyset\emptyset}^{(0)}=1\end{aligned}\end{cases}
(412)
for universal error detection, where  D_{\lambda}  for  \lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}  are defined by
\displaystyle D_{\lambda}\coloneqq\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\Tr_{\nu}(C_{\lambda\nu}),
(413)
C^{(i)}_{\lambda\kappa}  for  \lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{i},\kappa\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{i}  are defined by
\displaystyle C^{(i)}_{\lambda\kappa}\coloneqq\begin{cases}{1\over D}\sum_{\nu\in\kappa+_{D}\square}(\mathds{1}_{d_{\lambda}}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})C_{\lambda\nu}(\mathds{1}_{d_{\lambda}}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})^{\dagger}&(i=n)\\
{1\over D}\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+_{d}\square,\nu\in\kappa+_{D}\square}(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})C_{\mu\nu}^{(i+1)}(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})^{\dagger}&(0\leq i\leq n-1)\end{cases},
(414)
and  X^{\gamma}_{\lambda}  are defined in Eq. (
410
).
E.2
Conditions for isometry inversion, universal error detection, and isometry adjointation under the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry
We consider the action of a supermap  \mathcal{C}  on  n  calls of an isometry operation  V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)  when its Choi operator  C  satisfies the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry (
401
). Then,  \mathcal{C}(\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n})  is given in the following form.
Lemma 17
.
If the Choi operator of a quantum supermap  \mathcal{C} , denoted by  C , satisfies the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry (
401
), then  \mathcal{C}(\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n})  for  V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)  is given by
\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n})(\rho_{\mathrm{in}})=\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &xV_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}V_{\mathrm{in}}+{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}\Tr[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}(y\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}+z(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}))]\\
&\hskip 90.0pt(\mathrm{isometry\;inversion},\mathrm{isometry\;adjointation})\end{aligned}\\
\Tr[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}(v\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}+w(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}))]\hskip 24.0pt(\mathrm{universal\;error\;detection})\end{cases}
(415)
for all  V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)  and  \rho_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C}^{d}) , where  \Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}  and  (\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})  are orthogonal projectors onto the image  {\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}  of  V_{\mathrm{in}}  and its complement  ({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}})^{\perp} , and  x,y,z,v,w\in\mathbb{C}  are constant numbers given by
\displaystyle x
\displaystyle\coloneqq{1\over d^{2}-1}\Tr[C(d^{2}\Omega-\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})],
(416)
\displaystyle y
\displaystyle\coloneqq{d^{2}\over d^{2}-1}\Tr[C(\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}-\Omega)],
(417)
\displaystyle z
\displaystyle\coloneqq\Tr[C(\Sigma\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})],
(418)
for isometry inversion and isometry adjointation, and
\displaystyle v
\displaystyle\coloneqq\Tr(C\Xi),
(419)
\displaystyle w
\displaystyle\coloneqq\Tr(C\Sigma),
(420)
for universal error detection,  \Omega,\Xi,\Sigma  are defined by
\displaystyle\Omega
\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{i,j,k,l=1}^{d_{\mu}}[\Omega_{\mu}]_{ik,jl}(E^{\mu,d}_{ij})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes(E^{\mu,D}_{kl})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}},
(421)
\displaystyle\Xi
\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\lambda+_{d}\square}\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\lambda}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}[\Xi_{\lambda\nu}]_{ak,bl}(E^{\lambda,d}_{ab})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(E^{\nu,D}_{kl})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
(422)
\displaystyle\Sigma
\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\lambda+_{D}\square}\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\lambda}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}[\Sigma_{\lambda\nu}]_{ak,bl}(E^{\lambda,d}_{ab})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(E^{\nu,D}_{kl})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}},
(423)
and  \Omega_{\mu}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C}^{d_{\mu}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{d_{\mu}})  and  \Xi_{\lambda\nu},\Sigma_{\lambda\nu}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C}^{d_{\lambda}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{d_{\nu}})  are defined by
\displaystyle[\Omega_{\mu}]_{ik,jl}
\displaystyle\coloneqq{[\pi_{\mu}]_{ik}^{*}[\pi_{\mu}]_{lj}\over d^{2}m_{\mu}^{(D)}},
(424)
\displaystyle[\Xi_{\lambda\nu}]_{ak,bl}
\displaystyle\coloneqq{m_{\nu}^{(d)}\over dm_{\nu}^{(D)}m_{\lambda}^{(d)}}[\pi_{\nu}]^{*}_{a^{\lambda}_{\nu}k}[\pi_{\nu}]_{lb_{\nu}^{\lambda}},
(425)
\displaystyle[\Sigma_{\lambda\nu}]_{ak,bl}
\displaystyle\coloneqq{1\over D-d}\left[{1\over m_{\lambda}^{(D)}}-\delta_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}{m_{\nu}^{(d)}\over m_{\nu}^{(D)}m_{\lambda}^{(d)}}\right][\pi_{\nu}]^{*}_{a^{\lambda}_{\nu}k}[\pi_{\nu}]_{lb_{\nu}^{\lambda}},
(426)
where  [\pi_{\mu}]_{ij}  are matrix elements of the irreducible representation  \pi_{\mu}  for  \pi\coloneqq(12\cdots n+1)\in\mathfrak{S}_{n+1}  shown in Eq. (
55
) defined by  [\pi_{\mu}]_{ij}\coloneqq\bra{\mu,i}\pi_{\mu}\ket{\mu,j} ,  \delta_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}  is defined by  \delta_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}=1  for  \nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}  and  \delta_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}=0  for  \nu\notin\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1} , and  a^{\lambda}_{\nu}  is the index of the standard tableau  s_{a^{\lambda}_{\nu}}^{\nu}  obtained by adding a box  n+1  to the standard tableau  s^{\lambda}_{a} .
Proof.
First, we consider the Choi operator  C  satisfying
\displaystyle[C,U^{\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]=0
(427)
for all  U\in\mathbb{U}(d)  and  U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D) . Then,  C\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}  for  V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)  satisfies
\displaystyle C\star|{U^{\prime}V_{\mathrm{in}}U}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{U^{\prime}V_{\mathrm{in}}U}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
\displaystyle=C\star(U^{T\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes U^{\prime\otimes n}_{\mathcal{O}^{n}})|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}(U^{T\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes U^{\prime\otimes n}_{\mathcal{O}^{n}})^{\dagger}
(428)
\displaystyle=(U^{T\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes U^{\prime\otimes n}_{\mathcal{O}^{n}})^{T}C(U^{T\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes U^{\prime\otimes n}_{\mathcal{O}^{n}})^{*}\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
(429)
\displaystyle=(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes U^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}}\otimes U^{\prime*}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{O}^{n}})C(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes U^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}}\otimes U^{\prime*}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{O}^{n}})^{\dagger}\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
(430)
\displaystyle=(U^{\prime*}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes U^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}})[C\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}](U^{\prime*}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes U^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}})^{\dagger}
(431)
for all  U\in\mathbb{U}(d)  and  U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D) . For  U\in\mathbb{U}(d)  and  U^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{U}[({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}})^{\perp}] ,  U^{\prime}\coloneqq V_{\mathrm{in}}UV_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}+U^{\prime\prime}  is a unitary operator and  U^{\prime}V_{\mathrm{in}}U=V_{\mathrm{in}}  holds. By substituting  U  and  U^{\prime}=V_{\mathrm{in}}UV_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}+U^{\prime\prime}  to Eq. (
431
), we obtain
\displaystyle[(V_{\mathrm{in}}UV_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}+U^{\prime\prime})^{*}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes U^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}}][C\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}][(V_{\mathrm{in}}UV_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}+U^{\prime\prime})^{*}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes U^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}}]^{\dagger}=C\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}.
(432)
Decomposing  C\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}  as
\displaystyle C\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}=(V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})P(V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})^{\dagger}+(V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})Q+R(V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})^{\dagger}+S
(433)
using linear operators  P\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C}^{d}\otimes\mathcal{F}) ,  Q:({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}})^{\perp}\otimes\mathcal{F}\to\mathbb{C}^{d}\otimes\mathcal{F} ,  R:\mathbb{C}^{d}\otimes\mathcal{F}\to({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}})^{\perp}\otimes\mathcal{F} , and  S\in\mathcal{L}(({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}})^{\perp}\otimes\mathcal{F}) , Eq. (
432
) is written as
\displaystyle(U^{*}\otimes U)A(U^{*}\otimes U)^{\dagger}
\displaystyle=A,
(434)
\displaystyle(U^{*}\otimes U)B(U^{\prime\prime T}\otimes U)^{\dagger}
\displaystyle=B,
(435)
\displaystyle(U^{\prime\prime T}\otimes U)C(U^{*}\otimes U)^{\dagger}
\displaystyle=C,
(436)
\displaystyle(U^{\prime\prime T}\otimes U)D(U^{\prime\prime T}\otimes U)^{\dagger}
\displaystyle=D.
(437)
Therefore,  P  is written as a linear combination of  |{\mathds{1}_{d}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{\mathds{1}_{d}}|  and  \mathds{1}_{d}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d} ,  Q=0 ,  R=0 , and  S  is proportional to  \mathds{1}_{({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}})^{\perp}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}} . Therefore,  C\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}  can be expressed by three parameters  x_{V_{\mathrm{in}}} ,  y_{V_{\mathrm{in}}} , and  z_{V_{\mathrm{in}}}  as
\displaystyle C\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
\displaystyle=x_{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{F}}+y_{V_{\mathrm{in}}}(\Pi^{T}_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}+z_{V_{\mathrm{in}}}(\Pi^{T}_{({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}})^{\perp}})_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}.
(438)
From Eq. (
431
), we obtain
\displaystyle x_{U^{\prime}V_{\mathrm{in}}U}|{(U^{\prime}V_{\mathrm{in}}U)^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{(U^{\prime}V_{\mathrm{in}}U)^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{F}}+y_{U^{\prime}V_{\mathrm{in}}U}(\Pi^{T}_{{\mathrm{Im}}(U^{\prime}V_{\mathrm{in}}U)})_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}+z_{U^{\prime}V_{\mathrm{in}}U}(\Pi^{T}_{({\mathrm{Im}}(U^{\prime}V_{\mathrm{in}}U))^{\perp}})_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}
\displaystyle=(U^{\prime*}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes U^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}})[x_{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{F}}+y_{V_{\mathrm{in}}}(\Pi^{T}_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}+z_{V_{\mathrm{in}}}(\Pi^{T}_{({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}})^{\perp}})_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}](U^{\prime*}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes U^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{F}})^{\dagger}
(439)
\displaystyle=x_{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|{(U^{\prime}V_{\mathrm{in}}U)^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{(U^{\prime}V_{\mathrm{in}}U)^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{F}}+y_{V_{\mathrm{in}}}(\Pi^{T}_{{\mathrm{Im}}(U^{\prime}V_{\mathrm{in}}U)})_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}+z_{V_{\mathrm{in}}}(\Pi^{T}_{({\mathrm{Im}}(U^{\prime}V_{\mathrm{in}}U))^{\perp}})_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d},
(440)
thus,  x_{V_{\mathrm{in}}} ,  y_{V_{\mathrm{in}}}  and  z_{V_{\mathrm{in}}}  does not depend on  V_{\mathrm{in}} . By rewriting  x_{V_{\mathrm{in}}}=x ,  y_{V_{\mathrm{in}}}=y  and  z_{V_{\mathrm{in}}}=z , we obtain
\displaystyle C\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
\displaystyle=x|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{F}}+y(\Pi^{T}_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}+z(\Pi^{T}_{({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}})^{\perp}})_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}.
(441)
This Choi operator corresponds to the map
\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n})(\rho_{\mathrm{in}})=xV_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}\rho_{\mathrm{in}}V_{\mathrm{in}}+{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}\Tr[\rho_{\mathrm{in}}(y\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}+z(\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}))].
(442)
We calculate  x ,  y  and  z  as follows. Equation (
441
) is written as
\displaystyle\Tr_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}[C(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{F}}\otimes|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}})]
\displaystyle=x|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{F}}+y(\Pi^{T}_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}+z(\Pi^{T}_{({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}})^{\perp}})_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}.
(443)
Taking the Hilbert-Schmidt inner product with  |{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}|_{\mathcal{F}\mathcal{P}} ,  (\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})^{*}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}  and  ((\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}))^{*}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}} , we obtain
\displaystyle\Tr[C|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\otimes|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}|_{\mathcal{F}\mathcal{P}}]
\displaystyle=d^{2}x+y,
(444)
\displaystyle\Tr[C|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\otimes(\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})^{*}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}]
\displaystyle=d(x+y),
(445)
\displaystyle\Tr[C|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\otimes((\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}))^{*}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}]
\displaystyle=(D-d)z.
(446)
Taking the Haar integral  \differential V_{\mathrm{in}}  on  \mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D) , we obtain
\displaystyle\Tr(C\Omega)
\displaystyle=x+{y\over d^{2}},
(447)
\displaystyle\Tr[C(\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]
\displaystyle=x+y,
(448)
\displaystyle\Tr[C(\Sigma\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]
\displaystyle=z,
(449)
where  \Omega ,  \Xi  and  \Sigma  are defined by
\displaystyle\Omega
\displaystyle\coloneqq{1\over d^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}\differential V|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\otimes|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|_{\mathcal{F}\mathcal{P}},
(450)
\displaystyle\Xi
\displaystyle\coloneqq{1\over d}\int_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}\differential V|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\otimes(\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V})_{\mathcal{P}},
(451)
\displaystyle\Sigma
\displaystyle\coloneqq{1\over D-d}\int_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}\differential V|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\otimes((\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V}))_{\mathcal{P}}.
(452)
Therefore,  x ,  y  and  z  are given by
\displaystyle x
\displaystyle={1\over d^{2}-1}\Tr[C(d^{2}\Omega-\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})],
(453)
\displaystyle y
\displaystyle={d^{2}\over d^{2}-1}\Tr[C(\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}-\Omega)],
(454)
\displaystyle z
\displaystyle=\Tr[C(\Sigma\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})].
(455)
Next, we consider the Choi operator  C  satisfying
\displaystyle[C,U^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]=0
(456)
for all  U\in\mathbb{U}(d)  and  U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D) . Defining  C^{\prime}\coloneqq C\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d} ,  C^{\prime}  satisfies
\displaystyle[C^{\prime},U^{\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]=0
(457)
for all  U\in\mathbb{U}(d)  and  U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D) . Therefore, we can show that there exist constant numbers  u ,  v , and  w  such that
\displaystyle C\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}
\displaystyle=C^{\prime}\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
(458)
\displaystyle=u|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{\dagger}}|_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{F}}+v(\Pi^{T}_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d}+w(\Pi^{T}_{({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}})^{\perp}})_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\over d},
(459)
i.e.,  u=0  and
\displaystyle C\star|{V_{\mathrm{in}}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}=v(\Pi^{T}_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})_{\mathcal{P}}+w(\Pi^{T}_{({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}})^{\perp}})_{\mathcal{P}}
(460)
holds. We calculate  v  and  w  as follows. Equation (
460
) is written as
\displaystyle\Tr_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}[C(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}})]=v(\Pi^{T}_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})_{\mathcal{P}}+w(\Pi^{T}_{({\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}})^{\perp}})_{\mathcal{P}}.
(461)
Taking the Hilbert-Schmidt inner product with  \Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}^{*}  and  (\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})^{*} , we obtain
\displaystyle\Tr[C|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\otimes(\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}})^{*}_{\mathcal{P}}]
\displaystyle=dv,
(462)
\displaystyle\Tr[C|{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V_{\mathrm{in}}^{*}}|^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\otimes((\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}}}))^{*}_{\mathcal{P}}]
\displaystyle=(D-d)w.
(463)
Taking the Haar integral  \differential V_{\mathrm{in}}  on  \mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D) , we obtain
\displaystyle\Tr(C \Xi)
\displaystyle=v,
(464)
\displaystyle\Tr(C \Sigma)
\displaystyle=w.
(465)
We calculate  \Omega ,  \Xi  and  \Sigma  as follows. First, due to the left- and right-invariance of the Haar measure  \differential V  given by  \differential V=\differential(U'VU)  for all  U\in\mathbb{U}(d)  and  U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D) ,  \Omega  and  \Xi  satisfies the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry given by
\displaystyle[\Omega,U^{\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]
\displaystyle=0
(466)
for all  U\in\mathbb{U}(d)  and  U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D) .
Thus, they can be written as
\displaystyle\Omega
\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d_{\mu}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}\Omega^{\mu\nu}_{ijkl}(E^{\mu,d}_{ij})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes(E^{\nu,D}_{kl})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}},
(467)
using complex coefficients  \Omega^{\mu\nu}_{ijkl}\in\mathbb{C} . The coefficients can be calculated as
\displaystyle\Omega^{\mu\nu}_{ijkl}
\displaystyle={1\over m_{\mu}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}\Tr[\Omega(E^{\mu,d}_{ji})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes(E^{\nu,D}_{lk})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]
(468)
\displaystyle={1\over m_{\mu}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}\Tr[\Omega(E^{\mu,d}_{ji})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes(P_{\pi}^{\dagger}E^{\nu,D}_{lk}P_{\pi})_{\mathcal{O}^{n}\mathcal{P}}]
(469)
\displaystyle={1\over m_{\mu}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}\sum_{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}=1}^{d_{\nu}}{1\over d^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}\differential V\Tr[|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|^{\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{O}^{n}\mathcal{P}}(E^{\mu,d}_{ji})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes[\pi_{\nu}]_{ll^{\prime}}(E^{\nu,D}_{l^{\prime}k^{\prime}})_{\mathcal{O}^{n}\mathcal{P}}[\pi_{\nu}]_{k^{\prime}k}^{*}]
(470)
\displaystyle={1\over m_{\mu}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}\sum_{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}=1}^{d_{\nu}}{1\over d^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)}\differential V\Tr[|{\mathds{1}_{d}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{\mathds{1}_{d}}|^{\otimes n+1}(E^{\mu,d}_{ji})\otimes[\pi_{\nu}]_{ll^{\prime}}V^{\dagger\otimes n+1}(E^{\nu,D}_{l^{\prime}k^{\prime}})V^{\otimes n+1}[\pi_{\nu}]_{k^{\prime}k}^{*}],
(471)
where  P_{\pi}  is the permutation of Hilbert spaces defined in Eq. (
55
) for  \pi=(12\cdots n+1)\in\mathfrak{S}_{n+1} ,  [\pi_{\mu}]_{ij}  are matrix elements of the irreducible representation  \pi_{\mu}  defined by  [\pi_{\mu}]_{ij}\coloneqq\bra{\mu,i}\pi_{\mu}\ket{\mu,j} . As shown in Eq. (
201
) in Appendix
A.4
,  V^{\dagger\otimes n+1}(E^{\nu,D}_{l^{\prime}k^{\prime}})V^{\otimes n+1}  is given by  (E^{\nu,d}_{l^{\prime}k^{\prime}})\delta_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}} . We consider the Schur basis introduced in Section
3.1.2
. Since the quantum Schur transform is a real matrix, the maximally entangled state in the Schur basis is the same as that in the Schur basis, i.e.,
\displaystyle|{\mathds{1}_{d}}\rangle\!\rangle^{\otimes n+1}=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{u=1}^{m_{\mu}^{(d)}}\sum_{i=1}^{d_{\mu}}\ket{\mu,u}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mu}^{(d)}}\otimes\ket{\mu,i}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}}\otimes\ket{\mu,u}_{\mathcal{U}_{\mu}^{(d)}}\otimes\ket{\mu,i}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}}.
(472)
Thus,  \Omega^{\mu\nu}_{ijkl}  is further calculated as
\displaystyle\Omega^{\mu\nu}_{ijkl}
\displaystyle={\delta_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\over m_{\mu}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}\sum_{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}=1}^{d_{\nu}}{1\over d^{2}}\Tr[|{\mathds{1}_{d}}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{\mathds{1}_{d}}|^{\otimes n+1}(E^{\mu,d}_{ji})\otimes[\pi_{\nu}]_{ll^{\prime}}(E^{\nu,d}_{l^{\prime}k^{\prime}})[\pi_{\nu}]_{k^{\prime}k}^{*}]
(473)
\displaystyle={\delta_{\mu\nu}[\pi_{\mu}]_{ik}^{*}[\pi_{\mu}]_{lj}\over d^{2}m_{\mu}^{(D)}},
(474)
Therefore,  \Omega  is given by
\displaystyle\Omega
\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{i,j,k,l=1}^{d_{\mu}}{[\pi_{\mu}]_{ik}^{*}[\pi_{\mu}]_{lj}\over d^{2}m_{\mu}^{(D)}}(E^{\mu,d}_{ij})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes(E^{\mu,D}_{kl})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}.
(475)
Since  (\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V})_{\mathcal{P}}=\Tr_{\mathcal{F}}|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|_{\mathcal{F}\mathcal{P}}  holds,  \Xi  is calculated using Lemma
as
\displaystyle\Xi
\displaystyle=d\Tr_{\mathcal{F}}\Omega
(476)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\lambda\in\nu-\square}\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\lambda}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}{m_{\nu}^{(d)}\over dm_{\nu}^{(D)}m_{\lambda}^{(d)}}[\pi_{\nu}]_{a_{\nu}^{\lambda}k}^{*}[\pi_{\nu}]_{lb_{\nu}^{\lambda}}(E^{\lambda,d}_{ab})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(E^{\nu,D}_{kl})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}.
(477)
Since  ((\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V}))_{\mathcal{P}}=\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}}-(\Pi_{{\mathrm{Im}}V})_{\mathcal{P}}  holds,  \Sigma  is calculated using Lemma
as
\displaystyle\Sigma
\displaystyle={1\over D-d}\Bigg[\int\differential V|{V}\rangle\!\rangle\!\langle\!\langle{V}|_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}^{\otimes n}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}}-d\Xi\Bigg]
(478)
\displaystyle={1\over D-d}\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\lambda}}\Bigg[{(E_{ab}^{\lambda,d})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(E_{ab}^{\lambda,D})_{\mathcal{O}^{n}}\over m_{\lambda}^{(D)}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}}
\displaystyle\hskip 48.0pt-\sum_{\nu\in\lambda+_{d}\square}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}{m_{\nu}^{(d)}\over m_{\nu}^{(D)}m_{\lambda}^{(d)}}[\pi_{\mu}]_{a_{\nu}^{\lambda}k}^{*}[\pi_{\mu}]_{lb_{\nu}^{\lambda}}(E^{\lambda,d}_{ab})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(E^{\nu,D}_{kl})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\Bigg]
(479)
\displaystyle={1\over D-d}\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\lambda}}\sum_{\nu\in\lambda+_{D}\square}\Bigg[{(E_{ab}^{\lambda,d})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(P_{\pi}^{\dagger}E_{a^{\lambda}_{\nu}b^{\lambda}_{\nu}}^{\nu,D}P_{\pi})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\over m_{\lambda}^{(D)}}
\displaystyle\hskip 48.0pt-\delta_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}{m_{\nu}^{(d)}\over m_{\nu}^{(D)}m_{\lambda}^{(d)}}[\pi_{\nu}]_{a_{\nu}^{\lambda}k}^{*}[\pi_{\nu}]_{lb_{\nu}^{\lambda}}(E^{\lambda,d}_{ab})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(E^{\nu,D}_{kl})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\Bigg]
(480)
\displaystyle={1\over D-d}\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\lambda+_{D}\square}\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\lambda}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}\Bigg[{1\over m_{\lambda}^{(D)}}-\delta_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}{m_{\nu}^{(d)}\over m_{\nu}^{(D)}m_{\lambda}^{(d)}}\Bigg][\pi_{\nu}]_{a_{\nu}^{\lambda}k}^{*}[\pi_{\nu}]_{lb_{\nu}^{\lambda}}(E^{\lambda,d}_{ab})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(E^{\nu,D}_{kl})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
(481)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\lambda+_{D}\square}\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\lambda}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}{1\over m_{\nu}^{(D)}}{\mathrm{hook}(\lambda)\over\mathrm{hook}(\nu)}[\pi_{\nu}]_{a_{\nu}^{\lambda}k}^{*}[\pi_{\nu}]_{lb_{\nu}^{\lambda}}(E^{\lambda,d}_{ab})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(E^{\nu,D}_{kl})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}.
(482)
∎
The parameters  x,y,z,v,w  in Lemma
17
are related to the constraints and the figure of merit of each task considered in this work as follows:
•
Probabilistic exact isometry inversion:  x=p  and  y=0 .
•
Deterministic isometry inversion:  x+y/d^{2}=F_{\mathrm{worst}} .
•
Universal error detection:  v=1 ,  w=\alpha .
•
Isometry adjointation:  x+y=1 ,  \max\{1-x-y/d^{2},z\}=\epsilon .
Using this property, we derive the SDP to obtain the optimal transformations of isometry operations in the next section.
E.3
Derivation of the SDP to obtain optimal transformation of isometry operations
E.3.1
Probabilistic exact isometry inversion
From Theorem
8
and Appendix
E.2
, the optimization problem of the success probability for isometry inversion is formulated as follows:
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max\Tr(C_S\Omega)\\
\mathrm{s.t.}\;&0\leq C_{S},C_{F}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F}),\\
&C\coloneqq C_{S}+C_{F}\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)},\\
&\Tr(C_S\Omega)=\Tr[C_{S}(\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})],\\
&[C_{a},U^{\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]=0\quad\forall U\in\mathbb{U}(d),U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D),a\in\{S,F\}.\end{split}
(483)
Using the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry of  \{C_{S},C_{F}\} , we write  \{C_{S},C_{F}\}  similarly to Eq. (
402
) as
\displaystyle C_{S}
\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d_{\mu}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}\frac{[S_{\mu\nu}]_{ik,jl}}{m_{\mu}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}(E_{ij}^{\mu,d})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes(E_{kl}^{\nu,D})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}},
(484)
\displaystyle C_{F}
\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d_{\mu}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}\frac{[F_{\mu\nu}]_{ik,jl}}{m_{\mu}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}(E_{ij}^{\mu,d})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes(E_{kl}^{\nu,D})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}.
(485)
Then,  \Tr(C_S\Omega)  and  \Tr[C_{S}(\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]  are given by
\displaystyle\Tr(C_S \Omega)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\Tr(S_{\mu\mu}\Omega_\mu),
(486)
\displaystyle\Tr[C_{S}(\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]
\displaystyle=\Tr[\Tr_{\mathcal{F}}(C_{S})\Xi]
(487)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+_{d}\square}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\lambda}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}\frac{[S_{\mu\nu}]_{a^{\lambda}_{\mu}k,b^{\lambda}_{\mu}l}}{m_{\mu}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}\Tr[(E_{ab}^{\lambda,d})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(E_{kl}^{\nu,D})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}\Xi]
(488)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+_{d}\square}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\Tr[(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})S_{\mu\nu}(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})^{\dagger}\Xi_{\lambda\nu}],
(489)
where  X^{\lambda}_{\mu}  is defined in Eq. (
410
) and  a^{\lambda}_{\mu}  is the index of the standard tableau  s^{\mu}_{a^{\lambda}_{\mu}}  obtained by adding a box  n+1  to the standard tableau  s^{\lambda}_{a} . Therefore, the SDP (
483
) is written as
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\Tr(S_{\mu\mu}\Omega_\mu)\\
\text{s.t. }&0\leq S_{\mu\nu},F_{\mu\nu}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C}^{d_{\mu}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{d_{\nu}})\quad\forall\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1},\\
&\{C_{\mu\nu}\}\coloneqq\{S_{\mu\nu}+F_{\mu\nu}\}\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)}_{\mathrm{sym}},\\
&\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\Tr(S_{\mu\mu}\Omega_\mu)=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+_{d}\square}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\Tr[(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})S_{\mu\nu}(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})^{\dagger}\Xi_{\lambda\nu}].\end{split}
(490)
E.3.2
Deterministic isometry inversion
From Theorem
8
and Appendix
E.2
, the optimization problem of the fidelity of deterministic isometry inversion is formulated as follows:
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max\Tr(C\Omega)\\
\mathrm{s.t.}\;&0\leq C\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F}),\\
&C\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)},\\
&[C,U^{\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]=0\quad\forall U\in\mathbb{U}(d),U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D).\end{split}
(491)
Similarly to probabilistic isometry inversion, this SDP can be rewritten as follows:
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\Tr(C_{\mu\mu}\Omega_\mu)\\
\text{s.t. }&0\leq C_{\mu\nu}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C}^{d_{\mu}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{d_{\nu}})\quad\forall\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1},\\
&\{C_{\mu\nu}\}\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)}_{\mathrm{sym}}.\end{split}
(492)
E.3.3
Universal error detection
From Theorem
8
and Appendix
E.2
, the optimization problem of  \lambda  of universal error detection is formulated as follows:
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\min\Tr(C_I\Sigma)\\
\mathrm{s.t.}\;&0\leq C_{I},C_{O}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}),\\
&C\coloneqq C_{I}+C_{O}\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)},\\
&\Tr(C_I\Xi)=1,\\
&[C_{a},U^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]=0\quad\forall U\in\mathbb{U}(d),U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D),a\in\{I,O\}.\end{split}
(493)
Using the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry of  \{C_{I},C_{O}\} , we write  \{C_{I},C_{O}\}  similarly to Eq. (
403
) as
\displaystyle C_{I}
\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\mu}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}\frac{[I_{\lambda\nu}]_{ak,bl}}{m_{\lambda}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}(E_{ab}^{\lambda,d})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(E_{kl}^{\nu,D})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}},
(494)
\displaystyle C_{O}
\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\mu}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}\frac{[O_{\lambda\nu}]_{ak,bl}}{m_{\lambda}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}(E_{ab}^{\lambda,d})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(E_{kl}^{\nu,D})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}.
(495)
Then,  \Tr(C_I\Sigma)  and  \Tr(C_I\Xi)  are given by
\displaystyle\Tr(C_I\Sigma)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\Tr(I_{\lambda\nu}\Sigma_{\lambda\nu}),
(496)
\displaystyle\Tr(C_I\Xi)
\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\Tr(I_{\lambda\nu}\Xi_{\lambda\nu}).
(497)
Therefore, the SDP (
493
) is written as
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\min\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\Tr(I_{\lambda\nu}\Sigma_{\lambda\nu})\\
\text{s.t. }&0\leq I_{\lambda\nu},O_{\lambda\nu}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C}^{d_{\lambda}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{d_{\nu}})\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1},\\
&\{C_{\lambda\nu}\}\coloneqq\{I_{\lambda\nu}+O_{\lambda\nu}\}\in{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}_{\mathrm{sym}},\\
&\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\Tr(I_{\lambda\nu}\Xi_{\lambda\nu})=1.\end{split}
(498)
E.3.4
Isometry adjointation
From Theorem
8
and Appendix
E.2
, the optimization problem of  \epsilon  of isometry adjointation is formulated as follows:
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\min\max\{1-\Tr(C_I\Omega),\Tr[C_{I}(\Sigma\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]\}\\
\mathrm{s.t.}\;&0\leq C_{I},C_{O}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F}),\\
&C\coloneqq C_{I}+C_{O}\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)},\\
&\Tr[C_{I}(\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]=1,\\
&[C_{a},U^{\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]=0\quad\forall U\in\mathbb{U}(d),U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D),a\in\{I,O\}.\end{split}
(499)
Using the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry of  \{C_{I},C_{O}\} , we write  \{C_{I},C_{O}\}  similarly to Eq. (
402
) as
\displaystyle C_{I}
\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d_{\mu}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}\frac{[I_{\mu\nu}]_{ik,jl}}{m_{\mu}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}(E_{ij}^{\mu,d})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes(E_{kl}^{\nu,D})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}},
(500)
\displaystyle C_{O}
\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d_{\mu}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}\frac{[O_{\mu\nu}]_{ik,jl}}{m_{\mu}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}}(E_{ij}^{\mu,d})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes(E_{kl}^{\nu,D})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}.
(501)
Then, similarly for the case of probabilistic exact isometry inversion, the SDP (
499
) as follows:
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\min\max\Big\{1-\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\Tr(I_{\mu\mu}\Omega_\mu),\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+_{d}\square}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\Tr[(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})I_{\mu\nu}(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})^{\dagger}\Sigma_{\lambda\nu}]\Big\}\\
\text{s.t. }&0\leq I_{\mu\nu},O_{\mu\nu}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C}^{d_{\mu}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{d_{\nu}})\quad\forall\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1},\\
&\{C_{\mu\nu}\}\coloneqq\{I_{\mu\nu}+O_{\mu\nu}\}\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)}_{\mathrm{sym}},\\
&\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\mu\in\lambda+_{d}\square}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\Tr[(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})I_{\mu\nu}(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})^{\dagger}\Xi_{\lambda\nu}]=1.\end{split}
(502)
E.4
Derivation of the dual problems
We derive the dual problems of the SDPs to obtain the optimal transformations of isometry operations. Due to the strong duality, the dual problems gives the same optimal value as the corresponding primal problems. To this end, we first introduce the dual set of the Choi operators of quantum superchannels. Then, we derive the dual problems using the dual set. Finally, we simplify the derived dual problems using the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry.
E.4.1
Characterization of the dual processes
We define the dual set  \overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}  of  \mathcal{W}^{(x)}  for  x\in\{\mathrm{PAR},\mathrm{SEQ},\mathrm{GEN}\}  by
\displaystyle\overline{C}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}\Longleftrightarrow\Tr(C\overline{C})=1\quad\forall C\in\mathcal{W}^{(x)}.
(503)
Introducing the basis  \{\overline{C}_{j}\}  of  \overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)} , this relation leads to
\displaystyle\overline{C}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}\Longleftrightarrow\Tr(C\overline{C}_j)=1\quad\forall j.
(504)
As shown in Ref.
[
113
]
,  \overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}  are given by
\displaystyle\overline{C}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(\mathrm{PAR})}
\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\begin{cases}\overline{C}=W\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\\
\Tr_{\mathcal{O}^{n}}W=\Tr_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{O}^{n}}W\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}/\dim{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\\
\Tr W=\dim\mathcal{I}^{n}\end{cases},
(505)
\displaystyle\overline{C}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(\mathrm{SEQ})}
\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\begin{cases}\overline{C}=W\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\\
\Tr_{\mathcal{O}_{i}}W^{(i)}=\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{I}_{i}}\otimes W^{(i-1)},\quad\forall i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}\\
\Tr W=\dim\mathcal{I}^{n}\end{cases},
(506)
\displaystyle\overline{C}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(\mathrm{GEN})}
\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\begin{cases}\overline{C}=W\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}\\
\Tr_{\mathcal{O}_{i}}W=\Tr_{\mathcal{I}_{i}\mathcal{O}_{i}}W\otimes{\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{I}_{i}}}/{\dim{\mathcal{I}_{i}}}\quad\forall i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}\\
\Tr W=\dim{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\end{cases},
(507)
where  W^{(i)}  are defined by
\displaystyle W^{(i)}\coloneqq\begin{cases}W&(i=n)\\
\Tr_{\mathcal{I}_{i+1}\mathcal{O}_{i+1}}W^{(i+1)}/\dim{\mathcal{I}_{i+1}}\quad\forall i\in\{0,\ldots,n\}&(i\in\{0,\cdots,n-1\})\end{cases}.
(508)
We also introduce the set  \mathrm{Cone}[\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}]  of the dual sets  \overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}  defined by
\displaystyle\mathrm{Cone}[\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}]=\{\lambda\overline{C}|\lambda\in\mathbb{C},\overline{C}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}\}.
(509)
E.4.2
Probabilistic exact isometry inversion
We write down the dual problem of the SDP (
483
). To this end, we note that the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry does not change the optimal value of the SDP (
483
), so we can remove it when we consider the dual problem. Then, the SDP (
483
) corresponds to the following optimization problem:
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max\Tr(C_S\Omega)\\
\text{s.t. }&0\leq C_{S},C_{F}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F}),\\
&\Tr(C_S\Omega)=\Tr[C_{S}(\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})],\\
&\Tr[(C_{S}+C_{F})\overline{C}_{j}]=1,\quad\forall j.\end{split}
(510)
By introducing the Lagrange multipliers  \omega,\lambda_{j}\in\mathbb{R}  and  0\leq\Gamma_{S},\Gamma_{F}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F}) , we can write down the corresponding Lagrangian
\displaystyle L=
\displaystyle\Tr(C_S\Omega)+[\Tr[C_{S}(\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]-\Tr(C_S \Omega)]\omega+\Tr(C_S\Gamma_S)+\Tr(C_F\Gamma_F)
\displaystyle+\sum_{j}[1-\Tr[(C_{S}+C_{F})\overline{C}_{j}]]\lambda_{j}
(511)
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}+\Tr[C_{S}(\Omega-\omega\Omega+\omega\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}+\Gamma_{S}-\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j})]+\Tr[F(\Gamma_{F}-\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j})],
(512)
which gives the SDP (
510
) as the following optimization:
\displaystyle\max_{C_{S},C_{F}\geq 0}\min_{\omega,\lambda_{j}\in\mathbb{R},\Gamma_{S},\Gamma_{F}\geq 0}L.
(513)
This optimization problem corresponds to the following dual problem:
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\min\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\\
\text{s.t. }&\omega,\lambda_{j}\in\mathbb{R},0\leq\Gamma_{S},\Gamma_{F}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F}),\\
&(1-\omega)\Omega+\omega\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}+\Gamma_{S}-\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j}=0,\\
&\Gamma_{F}-\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j}=0.\end{split}
(514)
The variables  \Gamma_{S},\Gamma_{F}  can be removed, and the variables  \lambda_{j}  and  \overline{C}_{j}  can be replaced with  \overline{C}\coloneqq\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j} . Since  \Tr\overline{C}_{j}=d^{n+1}  holds for  \overline{C}_{j}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)} ,  \sum_{j}\lambda_{j}  can be replaced with  \Tr\overline{C}/d^{n+1} , which gives the following dual problem:
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\min\Tr\overline{C}/d^{n+1}\\
\text{s.t. }&\omega\in\mathbb{R},0\leq\overline{C}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F}),\\
&\overline{C}\in\mathrm{Cone}[\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}],\\
&\overline{C}\geq(1-\omega)\Omega+\omega\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}.\end{split}
(515)
E.4.3
Deterministic isometry inversion
Similarly to the case of probabilistic isometry inversion, the SDP (
491
) can be rewritten as the following optimization problem:
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max\Tr(C\Omega)\\
\text{s.t. }&0\leq C\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F}),\\
&\Tr[C\overline{C}_{j}]=1,\quad\forall j.\end{split}
(516)
By introducing the Lagrange multipliers  \lambda_{j}\in\mathbb{R}  and  0\leq\Gamma\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F}) , we can write down the corresponding Lagrangian
\displaystyle L
\displaystyle=\Tr(C\Omega)+\Tr(C\Gamma)+\sum_{j}[1-\Tr[C\overline{C}_{j}]]\lambda_{j}
(517)
\displaystyle=\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}+\Tr[C(\Omega+\Gamma-\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j})],
(518)
which gives the SDP (
491
) as the following optimization:
\displaystyle\max_{C\geq 0}\min_{\lambda_{j}\in\mathbb{R},\Gamma\geq 0}L.
(519)
This optimization problem corresponds to the following dual problem:
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\min\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\\
\text{s.t. }&\lambda_{j}\in\mathbb{R},0\leq\Gamma\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F}),\\
&\Omega+\Gamma-\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j}=0.\end{split}
(520)
The variable  \Gamma  can be removed, and the variables  \lambda_{j}  and  \overline{C}_{j}  can be replaced with  \overline{C}\coloneqq\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j}  as
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\min\Tr\overline{C}/d^{n+1}\\
\text{s.t. }&\overline{C}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F}),\\
&\overline{C}\in\mathrm{Cone}[\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}],\\
&\overline{C}\geq\Omega.\end{split}
(521)
E.4.4
Universal error detection
Similarly to the case of probabilistic isometry inversion, the SDP (
493
) can be rewritten as the following optimization problem:
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\min\Tr(C_I \Sigma)\\
\text{s.t. }&0\leq C_{I},C_{O}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}),\\
&\Tr(C_I \Xi)=1,\\
&\Tr[(C_{I}+C_{O})\overline{C}_{j}]=1\quad\forall j.\\
\end{split}
(522)
By introducing the Lagrange multipliers  \xi,\lambda_{j}\in\mathbb{R}  and  0\leq\Gamma_{I},\Gamma_{O}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}) , the corresponding Lagrangian is given by
\displaystyle L
\displaystyle=\Tr(C_I \Sigma)-\Tr(C_I\Gamma_I)-\Tr(C_O\Gamma_O)+[1-\Tr(C_I\Xi)]\xi+\sum_{j}[1-\Tr[(C_{I}+C_{O})\overline{C}_{j}]]\lambda_{j}
(523)
\displaystyle=\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}+\xi+\Tr[C_{I}\Big(\Sigma-\Gamma_{I}-\xi\Xi-\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j}\Big)\Bigg]+\Tr[C_{O}(-\Gamma_{O}-\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j})],
(524)
which gives the SDP (
493
) as the following optimization:
\displaystyle\min_{C_{I},C_{O}\geq 0}\max_{\xi,\lambda_{j}\in\mathbb{R},\Gamma_{I},\Gamma_{O}\geq 0}L.
(525)
The corresponding dual problem is given by
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}+\xi\\
\text{s.t. }&\Sigma-\Gamma_{I}-\xi\Xi-\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j}=0,\\
&-\Gamma_{O}-\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j}=0.\end{split}
(526)
The variable  \Gamma  can be removed, and the variables  \lambda_{j}  and  \overline{C}_{j}  can be replaced with  \overline{C}\coloneqq-\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j}  as
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max\xi-\Tr\overline{C}/d^{n}\\
\text{s.t. }&\xi\in\mathbb{R},0\leq\overline{C}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}),\\
&\overline{C}\in\mathrm{Cone}[\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}],\\
&\overline{C}\geq\xi\Xi-\Sigma.\end{split}
(527)
E.4.5
Isometry adjointation
Similarly to the case of probabilistic isometry inversion, the SDP (
499
) can be rewritten as the following optimization problem:
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\min p\\
\text{s.t. }&0\leq C_{I},C_{O}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F}),\\
&1-\Tr(C_I\Omega)\leq p,\\
&\Tr[C_{I}(\Sigma\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]\leq p\\
&\Tr[C_{I}(\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]=1,\\
&\Tr[(C_{I}+C_{O})\overline{C}_{j}]=1\quad\forall j.\end{split}
(528)
By introducing the Lagrange multipliers  \omega,\sigma\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0} ,  \xi,\lambda_{j}\in\mathbb{R}  and  0\leq\Gamma_{I},\Gamma_{O}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F}) , the corresponding Lagrangian is given by
\displaystyle L=
\displaystyle p-\Tr(C_I\Gamma_I)-\Tr(C_O\Gamma_O)+[1-\Tr(C_I\Omega)-p]\omega+[\Tr[C_{I}(\Sigma\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]-p]\sigma
\displaystyle+[1-\Tr[C_{I}(\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}})]]\xi+\sum_{j}[1-\Tr[(C_{I}+C_{O})\overline{C}_{j}]]\lambda_{j}
(529)
\displaystyle=
\displaystyle\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}+\omega+\xi+p(1-\omega-\sigma)+\Tr[C_{I}(-\Gamma_{I}-\omega\Omega+\sigma\Sigma\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}-\xi\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}-\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j})]
\displaystyle+\Tr[F(-\Gamma_{O}-\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j})].
(530)
The corresponding dual problem is given by
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}+\omega+\xi\\
\text{s.t. }&\omega+\sigma=1,\\
&-\Gamma_{I}-\omega\Omega+\sigma\Sigma\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}-\xi\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}-\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j}=0,\\
&-\Gamma_{O}-\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j}=0.\end{split}
(531)
The variables  \Gamma_{I},\Gamma_{O},\sigma  can be removed, and the variables  \lambda_{j}  and  \overline{C}_{j}  can be replaced with  \overline{C}\coloneqq-\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}\overline{C}_{j}  as
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max\omega+\xi-\Tr\overline{C}/d^{n+1}\\
\text{s.t. }&\xi\in\mathbb{R},0\leq\omega\leq 1,0\leq\overline{C}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{I}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{F})\\
&\overline{C}\in\mathrm{Cone}[\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}],\\
&\overline{C}\geq\omega\Omega+\xi\Xi\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}-(1-\omega)\Sigma\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}.\end{split}
(532)
E.4.6
Simplification of the dual problems using  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  and permutation symmetry
In the dual SDPs (
515
), (
521
), (
527
) and (
532
), we can impose the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry given by
\displaystyle\begin{cases}[\overline{C},U^{\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]=0&(\mathrm{isometry\;inversion},\mathrm{universal\;error\;detection})\\
[\overline{C},U^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes U^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}]=0&(\mathrm{isometry\;adjointation})\end{cases}
(533)
for all  U\in\mathbb{U}(d)  and  U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{U}(D) ,
since for the optimal  \overline{C}_{\mathrm{opt}} , the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D) -twirled operator  \overline{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{opt}}  given by
\displaystyle\overline{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{opt}}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U\mathcal{U}^{\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}(\overline{C})&(\mathrm{isometry\;inversion},\mathrm{universal\;error\;detection})\\
\int_{\mathbb{U}(d)}\differential U\mathcal{U}^{\otimes n}_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\prime\otimes n+1}_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}(\overline{C})&(\mathrm{isometry\;adjointation})\end{cases}
(534)
also gives the optimal values of the dual SDPs. For the case of  x=\mathrm{GEN} , we can also impose the permutation symmetry given by
\displaystyle\begin{cases}[\overline{C},(P_{\pi})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(P_{\pi})_{\mathcal{O}^{n}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}]=0&(\mathrm{isometry\;inversion},\mathrm{universal\;error\;detection})\\
[\overline{C},(P_{\pi})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(P_{\pi})_{\mathcal{O}^{n}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}}]=0&(\mathrm{isometry\;adjointation})\end{cases}
(535)
for all  \pi\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}  and  P_{\pi}  is given in Eq. (
55
) since the  \mathfrak{S}_{n} -twirled operator  \overline{C}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{opt}}  given by
\displaystyle\overline{C}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{opt}}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\sum_{\pi\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(\mathcal{P}_{\pi})_{\mathcal{O}^{n}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{C})&(\mathrm{isometry\;inversion},\mathrm{universal\;error\;detection})\\
\sum_{\pi\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}(\mathcal{P}_{\pi})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(\mathcal{P}_{\pi})_{\mathcal{O}^{n}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{P}}(\overline{C})&(\mathrm{isometry\;adjointation})\end{cases}
(536)
also gives the optimal values of the dual SDPs.
We characterize the set  \overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}  under the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry (
533
) [and the permutation symmetry (
535
) for  x=\mathrm{GEN} ]. Using the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  symmetry (
533
), we write  \overline{C}  using the operator  E^{\mu,d}_{ij}  introduced in Eq. (
60
) as
\displaystyle\overline{C}=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d_{\mu}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}[\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}]_{ik,jl}(E_{ij}^{\mu,d})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}}\otimes(E_{kl}^{\nu,D})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
(537)
for isometry inversion or isometry adjointation, and
\displaystyle\overline{C}=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\lambda}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}[\overline{C}_{\lambda\nu}]_{ak,bl}(E_{ab}^{\lambda,d})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(E_{kl}^{\nu,D})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}}
(538)
for universal error detection using a  d_{\mu}d_{\nu}  ( d_{\lambda}d_{\nu} )-dimensional square matrix  \overline{C}_{\mu\nu}  ( \overline{C}_{\lambda\nu} ), where  ik  ( ak ) and  jl  ( bl ) are the indices for row and column numbers, respectively. We also write  W  and  W^{(i)}  appearing in the characterization of  \overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}  as
\displaystyle W
\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\lambda}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}[W_{\lambda\nu}]_{ak,bl}(E^{\lambda}_{ab})_{\mathcal{I}^{n}}\otimes(E^{\nu}_{kl})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{n}},
(539)
\displaystyle W^{(i)}
\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{i}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{i+1}}\sum_{a,b=1}^{d_{\lambda}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{d_{\nu}}[W^{(i)}_{\lambda\nu}]_{ak,bl}(E^{\lambda}_{ab})_{\mathcal{I}^{i}}\otimes(E^{\nu}_{kl})_{\mathcal{P}\mathcal{O}^{i}}.
(540)
Using Lemma
, the condition  \overline{C}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}  for  x\in\{\mathrm{PAR},\mathrm{SEQ},\mathrm{GEN}\}  are given by
\displaystyle\overline{C}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}\Longleftrightarrow\begin{cases}\{\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}\}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(x)}&(\mathrm{isometry\;inversion},\mathrm{isometry\;adjointation})\\
\{\overline{C}_{\lambda\nu}\}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(x)}&(\mathrm{universal\;error\;detection})\end{cases},
(541)
where  \overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(x)}  is given by
\displaystyle\{\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}\}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(\mathrm{PAR})}\Longleftrightarrow
\displaystyle\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}=\sum_{\lambda\in\mu-\square}(X^{\mu}_{\lambda}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})W_{\lambda\nu}(X^{\mu}_{\lambda}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})^{\dagger}\quad\forall\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}\\
&\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}m_{\nu}^{(D)}\Tr_{\nu}(W_{\lambda\nu})=\mathds{1}_{d_{\lambda}}\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}\end{aligned}\end{cases},
(542)
\displaystyle\{\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}\}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(\mathrm{SEQ})}\Longleftrightarrow
\displaystyle\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}=\sum_{\lambda\in\mu-\square}(X^{\mu}_{\lambda}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})W_{\lambda\nu}(X^{\mu}_{\lambda}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})^{\dagger}\quad\forall\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}\\
&\begin{aligned} \sum_{\nu\in\kappa+_{D}\square}m_{\nu}^{(D)}(\mathds{1}_{d_{\lambda}}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})W^{(i)}_{\lambda\nu}(\mathds{1}_{d_{\lambda}}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})^{\dagger}=\sum_{\gamma\in\lambda-\square}m_{\kappa}^{(D)}(X^{\lambda}_{\gamma}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\kappa}})W^{(i-1)}_{\gamma\kappa}(X^{\lambda}_{\gamma}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\kappa}})^{\dagger}\\
\forall i\in\{1,\cdots,n\},\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{i},\kappa\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{i}\end{aligned}\\
&W^{(0)}_{\emptyset\square}={1\over D}\end{aligned}\end{cases},
(543)
\displaystyle\{\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}\}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(\mathrm{GEN})}\Longleftrightarrow
\displaystyle\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}=\sum_{\lambda\in\mu-\square}(X^{\mu}_{\lambda}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})W_{\lambda\nu}(X^{\mu}_{\lambda}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})^{\dagger}\quad\forall\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}\\
&[W_{\lambda\nu},\pi_{\lambda}\otimes\pi^{\prime}_{\nu}]=0\quad\forall\pi\in\mathfrak{S}_{n},\\
&\begin{aligned} \sum_{\nu\in\kappa+_{D}\square}m_{\nu}^{(D)}(\mathds{1}_{d_{\lambda}}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})W_{\lambda\nu}(\mathds{1}_{d_{\lambda}}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})^{\dagger}=\sum_{\gamma\in\lambda-\square}m_{\kappa}^{(D)}(X^{\lambda}_{\gamma}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\kappa}})W^{(n-1)}_{\gamma\kappa}(X^{\lambda}_{\gamma}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\kappa}})^{\dagger}\\
\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n},\kappa\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n}\end{aligned}\\
&\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}m_{\lambda}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}\Tr(W_{\lambda\nu})=d^{n}\end{aligned}\end{cases}
(544)
for isometry inversion and isometry adjointation, where  W^{(i)}_{\gamma\kappa}  for  \gamma\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{i}  and  \kappa\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{i+1}  are defined by
\displaystyle W^{(i)}_{\gamma\kappa}\coloneqq\begin{cases}W_{\gamma\kappa}&(i=n)\\
{1\over d}\sum_{\lambda\in\gamma+_{d}\square}\sum_{\nu\in\kappa+_{D}\square}{m_{\lambda}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}\over m_{\gamma}^{(d)}m_{\kappa}^{(D)}}(X^{\gamma}_{\lambda}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})W_{\lambda\nu}^{(i+1)}(X^{\gamma}_{\lambda}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})^{\dagger}&(i\in\{0,\cdots,n-1\})\end{cases},
(545)
\pi_{\lambda}  is the irreducible representation of  \pi  given in Eq. (
55
), and  \pi^{\prime}_{\nu}  is the irreducible representation of  \pi^{\prime}  defined by  \pi^{\prime}(1)=1  and  \pi^{\prime}(i+1)=\pi(i)+1  for  i\in\{1,\ldots,n\} . The set  \overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(x)}  is given by
\displaystyle\{\overline{C}_{\lambda\nu}\}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(\mathrm{PAR})}\Longleftrightarrow
\displaystyle\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &\overline{C}_{\lambda\nu}=W_{\lambda\nu}\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}\\
&\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}m_{\nu}^{(D)}\Tr_{\nu}(W_{\lambda\nu})=\mathds{1}_{d_{\lambda}}\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}\end{aligned}\end{cases},
(546)
\displaystyle\{\overline{C}_{\lambda\nu}\}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(\mathrm{SEQ})}\Longleftrightarrow
\displaystyle\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &\overline{C}_{\lambda\nu}=W_{\lambda\nu}\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}\\
&\begin{aligned} \sum_{\nu\in\kappa+_{D}\square}m_{\nu}^{(D)}(\mathds{1}_{d_{\lambda}}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})W^{(i)}_{\lambda\nu}(\mathds{1}_{d_{\lambda}}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})^{\dagger}=\sum_{\gamma\in\lambda-\square}m_{\kappa}^{(D)}(X^{\lambda}_{\gamma}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\kappa}})W^{(i-1)}_{\gamma\kappa}(X^{\lambda}_{\gamma}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\kappa}})^{\dagger}\\
\forall i\in\{1,\cdots,n\},\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{i},\kappa\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{i}\end{aligned}\\
&W^{(0)}_{\emptyset\square}={1\over D}\end{aligned}\end{cases},
(547)
\displaystyle\{\overline{C}_{\lambda\nu}\}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(\mathrm{GEN})}\Longleftrightarrow
\displaystyle\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} &\overline{C}_{\lambda\nu}=W_{\lambda\nu}\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}\\
&[W_{\lambda\nu},\pi_{\lambda}\otimes\pi^{\prime}_{\nu}]=0\quad\forall\pi\in\mathfrak{S}_{n},\\
&\begin{aligned} \sum_{\nu\in\kappa+_{D}\square}m_{\nu}^{(D)}(\mathds{1}_{d_{\lambda}}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})W_{\lambda\nu}(\mathds{1}_{d_{\lambda}}\otimes X^{\kappa}_{\nu})^{\dagger}=\sum_{\gamma\in\lambda-\square}m_{\kappa}^{(D)}(X^{\lambda}_{\gamma}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\kappa}})W^{(n-1)}_{\gamma\kappa}(X^{\lambda}_{\gamma}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\kappa}})^{\dagger}\\
\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n},\kappa\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n}\end{aligned}\\
&\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}m_{\lambda}^{(d)}m_{\nu}^{(D)}\Tr(W_{\lambda\nu})=d^{n}\end{aligned}\end{cases}
(548)
for universal error detection. Using this characterization of  \overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}  with the  \mathbb{U}(d)\times\mathbb{U}(D)  and permutation symmetry, the dual SDPs (
515
), (
521
), (
527
) and (
532
) can be simplified as follows:
•
Probabilistic exact isometry inversion
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\min\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\Tr\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}/d^{n+1}\\
\text{s.t. }&\omega\in\mathbb{R},0\leq\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C}^{d_{\mu}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{d_{\nu}})\quad\forall\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1},\\
&\{\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}\}\in\mathrm{Cone}[\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}_{\mathrm{sym}}],\\
&\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}\geq\delta_{\mu\nu}(1-\omega)\Omega_{\mu}+\omega\sum_{\lambda\in\mu-\square}(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\mu}})^{\dagger}\Xi_{\lambda\nu}(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\mu}})\quad\forall\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}.\end{split}
(549)
•
Deterministic isometry inversion
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\min\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\Tr\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}/d^{n+1}\\
\text{s.t. }&\omega\in\mathbb{R},0\leq\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C}^{d_{\mu}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{d_{\nu}})\quad\forall\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1},\\
&\{\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}\}\in\mathrm{Cone}[\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}_{\mathrm{sym}}],\\
&\overline{C}_{\mu\mu}\geq\Omega_{\mu}\quad\forall\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}.\end{split}
(550)
•
Universal error detection
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max\xi-\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\Tr\overline{C}_{\lambda\nu}/d^{n}\\
\text{s.t. }&\xi\in\mathbb{R},0\leq\overline{C}_{\lambda\nu}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C}^{d_{\lambda}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{d_{\nu}})\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1},\\
&\{\overline{C}_{\lambda\nu}\}\in\mathrm{Cone}[\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}_{\mathrm{sym}}],\\
&\overline{C}_{\lambda\nu}\geq\xi\Xi_{\lambda\nu}-\Sigma_{\lambda\nu}\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}.\end{split}
(551)
•
Isometry adjointation
\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max\omega+\xi-\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1}}\sum_{\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}}\Tr\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}/d^{n+1}\\
\text{s.t. }&\xi\in\mathbb{R},0\leq\omega\leq 1,0\leq\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C}^{d_{\mu}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{d_{\nu}})\quad\forall\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1},\\
&\{\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}\}\in\mathrm{Cone}[\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(x)}_{\mathrm{sym}}],\\
&\overline{C}_{\mu\nu}\geq\delta_{\mu\nu}\omega\Omega+\sum_{\lambda\in\mu-\square}(X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})^{\dagger}[\xi\Xi_{\lambda\nu}-(1-\omega)\Sigma_{\lambda\nu}](X^{\lambda}_{\mu}\otimes\mathds{1}_{d_{\nu}})\quad\forall\mu\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n+1},\nu\in\mathbb{Y}^{D}_{n+1}.\end{split}
(552)
Appendix F
Proof of Corollary
15
: Program cost of universal programming of two-outcome projective measurement
The proof of Theorem
12
shown in Appendix
B.2
constructs the parallel universal error detection protocol with the input state  \phi={\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{U}_{\lambda}^{(d)}}\over m_{\lambda}^{(d)}}\otimes\outerproduct{\mathrm{arb}}{\mathrm{arb}}_{\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}}  for an arbitrary state  \ket{\mathrm{arb}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}  and the POVM measurement  \mathcal{P}=\{\Pi^{(d)},\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi^{(d)}\} , where  \lambda  is defined in Eq. (
297
), and  \Pi^{(d)}  is given by
\displaystyle\Pi^{(d)}\coloneqq\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Y}^{d}_{n}}\Pi_{\lambda}^{(D)}
(553)
using the Young projector  \Pi_{\lambda}^{(d)}  defined in Eq. (
63
).
In the discussion below, we rewrite  \lambda  as  \lambda^{(d)}  to explicitly denote the dependency on  d .
The parallel protocol achieves the approximation error  \alpha=O(d^{2}n^{-1}) .
By using this protocol, we can implement the universal programming of rank- d  projective measurement  \mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{PVM}}^{(d)}=\{\Pi,\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi\}  with the program state  \phi_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{PVM}}^{(d)}}  given by
\displaystyle\phi_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{PVM}}^{(d)}}\coloneqq\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{in}}^{\otimes n}(\phi)
(554)
using  V_{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{V}_{\mathrm{iso}}(d,D)  such that  \Pi  is the orthogonal projector onto  {\mathrm{Im}}V_{\mathrm{in}} .
The approximation error is given by  \delta=\alpha=O(d^{2}n^{-1}) , and the program cost is given by
\displaystyle C_{\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{PVM}}^{(d,D)}}
\displaystyle=\log\dim\mathcal{U}_{\lambda^{(d)}}^{(D)}.
(555)
We evaluate the dimension of  \mathcal{U}_{\lambda^{(d)}}^{(D)}  by using Eq. (III.10) of Ref.
[
114
]
as follows:
\displaystyle\dim\mathcal{U}_{\lambda^{(d)}}^{(D)}
\displaystyle={\prod_{1\leq i
(556)
\displaystyle={\prod_{1\leq i
(557)
\displaystyle\leq{\prod_{1\leq i
(558)
\displaystyle={\prod_{1\leq i\leq d,d
(559)
\displaystyle\leq(kD)^{d(D-d)}
(560)
\displaystyle\leq\left({Dn\over d}\right)^{d(D-d)}
(561)
Thus, we obtain
\displaystyle C_{\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{PVM}}^{(d,D)}}
\displaystyle\leq d(D-d)\log\left({Dn\over d}\right)
(562)
\displaystyle\leq d(D-d)\log\left(\Theta(Dd)\over\delta\right).
(563)
We can also implement universal programming of two-outcome POVM measurement  \mathcal{M}=\{\Pi,\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi\}  as follows.
Since  0\leq\Pi\leq\mathds{1}_{D}  holds,  \Pi  has an eigendecomposition given by
\displaystyle\Pi=\sum_{j=1}^{D}a_{j}\outerproduct{\psi_j}{\psi_j},
(564)
where  0\leq a_{1}\leq\cdots\leq a_{D}\leq 1  and  \{\ket{\psi_j}\}_{j}  is an orthonormal basis of  \mathbb{C}^{D} .
Defining rank- d  projective measurement  \mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{PVM}}^{(d)}\coloneqq\{\Pi^{(d)},\mathds{1}_{D}-\Pi^{(d)}  by  \Pi^{(d)}\coloneqq\sum_{j=1}^{d}\outerproduct{\psi_j}{\psi_j}  for  d\in\{1,\ldots,D\}  and  \mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{PVM}}^{(0)}\coloneqq\{0,\mathds{1}_{D}\} , the POVM  \mathcal{M}  is given by a probabilistic mixture of  \{\mathcal{M}^{(d)}\}_{d=0}^{D}  as
[
104
,
103
]
\displaystyle\mathcal{M}=\sum_{d=0}^{D}p_{d}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{PVM}}^{(d)},
(565)
where  p_{d}  is given by  p_{0}=1-a_{D} ,  p_{1}=a_{1}  and  p_{d}=a_{d}-a_{d-1}  for  d\in\{2,\ldots,D\} .
Then, we can implement the program state of  \mathcal{M}  by
\displaystyle\phi_{\mathcal{M}}\coloneqq\sum_{d=0}^{D}p_{j}\outerproduct{d}{d}\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{PVM}}^{(d)}},
(566)
where  \phi_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{PVM}}^{(d)}}  is given in Eq. (
554
) for  d\in\{1,\ldots,D\}  and  \phi_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{PVM}}^{(0)}}  can be taken as an arbitrary state.
The approximation error is given by
\displaystyle\delta\leq\sum_{d=0}^{D}p_{d}O(d^{2}n^{-1})=O(D^{2}n^{-1})
(567)
and the program cost is evaluated as
\displaystyle C_{\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{PVM}}^{(D)}}
\displaystyle=\log\left(1+\sum_{d=1}^{D}\dim\mathcal{U}_{\lambda^{(d)}}^{(D)}\right)
(568)
\displaystyle\leq\log(D+1)+\max_{1\leq d\leq D}C_{\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{PVM}}^{(d,D)}}
(569)
\displaystyle\leq\log(D+1)+\lfloor D/2\rfloor(D-\lfloor D/2\rfloor)\log{\Theta(D^2)\over\delta}
(570)
\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}{D^{2}\over 4}\log{\Theta(D^2)\over\delta}&(D\mathrm{\;is\;even})\\
{D^{2}-1\over 4}\log{\Theta(D^2)\over\delta}&(D\mathrm{\;is\;odd})\\
\end{cases}.
(571)
